The implementation claims:
- ExprPlan gains Array(Vec<Vec<ExprPlan>>) and Error(ErrorValue) variants.
- Binder lowers Expr::Array element-wise with uniform-arity + cell-kind validation.
- Binder lowers Expr::Error mechanically.
- Defense-in-depth: BindError::ArrayRowArityMismatch and ArrayCellNotLiteral cover direct-AST construction that bypassed parser checks.
- Empty-rows Expr::Array surfaces as BindError::UnsupportedVariant — parser does not emit, only direct construction reaches this.
- ExprPlan::Array in scalar eval context returns Value::Error(Calc); cell-boundary spill path is deferred to 4.7.J.
- ExprPlan::Error in scalar eval returns Value::Error(ev).
- walk_plan_for_deps treats both new variants as leaf literals with no deps.
- 13 new tests passing, 2078 workspace tests total.

Verify each claim. For each:
- PASS / FLAG verdict + one-sentence justification.
- For FLAG: HIGH/MEDIUM/LOW + concrete file.rs:line citation + suggested fix.

Specifically scrutinize:
1. Does the binder rejection of array cells with non-literal Expr match the parser rejection set exactly, or are there mismatches? Specifically check whether the binder accepts Expr::NameRef inside an array cell when the parser would reject it.
2. The empty-rows Expr::Array path returns BindError::UnsupportedVariant with a hand-written string. Is that the right error class, or should there be a dedicated EmptyArrayLiteral variant parallel to ParseError::EmptyArrayLiteral?
3. Cell-boundary scalar-context contract: the design says #CALC! in scalar context. The W5-99 evaluator returns #CALC! for every ExprPlan::Array evaluation. Is this a sound interim state — will the spill-path detection in 4.7.J correctly INTERCEPT before reaching eval_scalar, or could a buggy spill-detection path lead to silent #CALC! at the cell boundary?
4. The variant_kind helper in plan.rs covers all 12 Expr variants. Verify exhaustiveness — if a future Expr variant lands, the binder array-cell rejection path would silently fall through. Should this be a match with explicit exhaustiveness?
5. ExprPlan::Array fingerprint: the calcgraph fingerprint module hashes Expr, not ExprPlan, so no new tag byte needed. Verify that ExprPlan changes do NOT affect the existing fingerprint stability test.
6. Does the binder fold any optimization that would silently re-shape the array? For example flatten 1x1 to a scalar, or collapse single-row 1xN to a Vec<ExprPlan>. Per design § 5 the binder is element-wise; verify no optimization sneaks in.
7. walk_plan_for_deps treats ExprPlan::Array as no-deps. Per design § 3.3 array cells are restricted to literals, so this is correct today. But will it be correct after Phase 4.10 lifts the restriction to allow refs inside array literals? File as a known-gap or note?

    W5-99 / Engine Phase 4.7.F — Binder Expr::Array + Expr::Error lowering
    
    Sixth implementation sub-phase of Phase 4.7. Lowers the parser's
    `Expr::Array(Vec<Vec<Expr>>)` + `Expr::Error(ErrorValue)` into new
    `ExprPlan` variants that the runtime + calcgraph already handle.
    
    New `ExprPlan` variants in `crates/ql-exec/src/plan.rs`:
    - `ExprPlan::Array(Vec<Vec<ExprPlan>>)` — row-major, uniform arity.
      Inner cells restricted to literal-kind plans (Number / Bool /
      String / Error) per design § 3.3.
    - `ExprPlan::Error(ErrorValue)` — error-sigil literal. Mechanical
      lowering from `Expr::Error(_)`.
    
    New `BindError` variants (defense in depth on the public AST):
    - `ArrayRowArityMismatch { expected, found, row }` — ragged Array.
      Parser already rejects via `ParseError::ArrayRowArityMismatch`,
      but `Expr::Array` is a public variant — tests/tools can construct
      ragged shapes directly. Codex MEDIUM-5 in the W5-94 design review.
    - `ArrayCellNotLiteral { row, col, got }` — non-literal cell. Parser
      rejects at parse time via `InvalidArrayCellToken`; binder mirror
      for direct AST construction. Includes a `variant_kind` helper for
      human-readable variant tags in the error.
    
    Binder lowering in `bind_with_context`:
    - `Expr::Error(ev)` → `ExprPlan::Error(ev)`.
    - `Expr::Array(rows)` → `ExprPlan::Array(bound_rows)` element-wise.
      Validates uniform row arity + restricted cell-kind set. Zero-row
      AST surfaces as `BindError::UnsupportedVariant`.
    
    Downstream `ExprPlan` callers updated:
    - `calcgraph_session::walk_plan_for_deps` — `Array` and `Error`
      have no dependencies (pure leaf literals).
    - `scalar::eval_scalar` AND `scalar::eval_scalar_with_cache` —
      `Error(ev)` returns `Value::Error(ev)`; `Array(_)` in scalar
      context returns `Value::Error(ErrorValue::Calc)` per design § 6.3
      (Codex HIGH-2). Cell-boundary spill path is W5-102 / Phase 4.7.J;
      until then, every array-in-scalar evaluation produces #CALC!.
    
    Tests (+13 from 2065 → 2078):
    - 8 binder tests (plan.rs):
      - Error literal lowering.
      - Array literal element-wise lowering (4 cell kinds).
      - Ragged-array → ArrayRowArityMismatch.
      - Non-literal cell → ArrayCellNotLiteral (CellRef + nested Array).
      - Zero-row → UnsupportedVariant.
      - 1x1 + 3x2 array lowering.
    - 5 eval-site tests (scalar.rs):
      - ExprPlan::Error → Value::Error via both eval entry points.
      - ExprPlan::Array → Value::Error(Calc) via both eval entry points.
      - 1 + #N/A end-to-end binary propagation.

503:`SEQUENCE(rows, [cols], [start], [step])` — generates a `rows × cols` array of arithmetic-progression numbers.
511:**Argument-error precedence (left-to-right):** `#VALUE!` if any arg fails coercion; `#NUM!` if `rows < 1` or `cols < 1`. Otherwise produces an array.
521:`FILTER(array, include, [if_empty])` — applies a boolean mask along the first axis.
524:- `array` (required, FunctionArg::Array or ::Range — both 2D shapes).
525:- `include` (required, same shape OR 1D matching one axis of `array`):
526:  - If `array` is N×M and `include` is N×1: filter rows where include[i]=TRUE.
527:  - If `array` is N×M and `include` is 1×M: filter columns where include[j]=TRUE.
528:  - If `include` is N×M (same shape as array): cell-wise filter — Phase 4.10 (out of scope for 4.7).
529:- `if_empty` (optional, scalar): returned if no `include` cell is TRUE; default is `#CALC!`.
531:**Argument-error precedence:** `#VALUE!` on shape mismatch (include axis doesn't match either array axis); `#CALC!` if no TRUE values AND no `if_empty` provided; result with `if_empty` value as a 1×1 array if all-FALSE AND `if_empty` provided.
538:- `FILTER({1;2;3}, {FALSE;FALSE;FALSE})` → `#CALC!`.
543:`TRANSPOSE(array)` — swaps rows/cols. Closes the array-eval surface symmetrically.
546:- `array` (required, FunctionArg::Array or ::Range).
550:**Argument-error precedence:** `#VALUE!` on non-array input (e.g. a scalar `Number`). No error for degenerate inputs (transpose of 0×N is N×0).

## 5. Binder — `ExprPlan::Array`

New variant:

```rust
ExprPlan::Array(Vec<Vec<ExprPlan>>)
```

Binder rule: `Expr::Array` → `ExprPlan::Array` element-wise. Each element binds to a `Number` / `Bool` / `String` / `Error` plan (no `Unary` — the lexer folds signs into literals).

**Codex MEDIUM:** `Expr::Array` is a PUBLIC AST variant. Tests + tools can construct invalid ASTs (e.g. ragged shapes via `Vec::push`). The binder MUST reject ragged ASTs with a clean error, not panic:

```rust
BindError::ArrayRowArityMismatch { expected: u32, found: u32, row: u32 }
```

This shadows the equivalent `ParseError` for the case where the AST was constructed directly. No `BindError::UnsupportedVariant` for `Expr::Array` itself — it's now supported.

 succeeded in 0ms:
### 3.2 Element-arity rule

All rows must have the same number of cells. Mismatched-row literals (e.g. `{1, 2; 3}`) error at parse time with `ParseError::ArrayRowArityMismatch { expected, found, row }`. **This is a Quantbook v1 subset** — Excel pads with `#N/A` for missing cells, and `{1,2;3}` evaluates as `{1,2;3,#N/A}`. Quantbook v1 chooses loud rejection over silent padding; the pad-with-`#N/A` variant is a Phase 4.10 polish item gated on test-corpus completeness.

### 3.3 Element kinds restricted in v1

Array cells in v1 — the allowed `array_cell` subset:

- `NUMBER` (positive or negative literal)
- `STRING` literal
- `BOOL` literal (`TRUE` / `FALSE`)
- `error literal` — `#NULL!`, `#DIV/0!`, `#VALUE!`, `#REF!`, `#NAME?`, `#NUM!`, `#N/A`, `#SPILL!`, `#CALC!` — the nine Excel-canon error sigils available as `ErrorValue::ALL` entries in `ql-types`. Needed for `FILTER(_, _, #N/A)` etc. (W5-97 closure / Sonnet M2: `#GETTING_DATA` was listed in the original draft but is not in `ErrorValue::ALL` — it's an Excel-Online-specific volatile data-wait state that we don't model in v1; lifting it would require adding the variant alongside `Disconnected`/`Timeout`. Deferred to Phase 4.10 or whenever a real use case appears.)

NO nested CellRef / RangeRef / Function calls / Unary operators (other than the implicit sign on numeric literals). Codex MEDIUM finding: explicitly document this as a Quantbook v1 subset.

Phase 4.10 lifts to allow refs (`{A1, B1; A2, B2}`) once the array evaluator and dependency extraction prove stable.

**Rationale:** array literals with refs would require shape determination at bind/eval time, not parse time, AND would entangle the array-literal grammar with the dependency graph. Restricting v1 to constants keeps the parser simple and the test corpus closed.

## 4. AST

`Expr::Array(Vec<Vec<Expr>>)` is already shape-locked. The parser populates it directly. Each inner `Expr` is constrained to the kinds in § 3.3.

**`Expr::Spill(Box<Expr>)` terminology note** (Codex LOW): the existing shape-lock at `ast.rs:67` is RESERVED for Excel's `A1#` spill-range-ref SYNTAX (Phase 4.9), NOT the runtime spill anchor. Excel's `A1#` means "the spill range anchored at A1" — it's a reference operator, not an anchor marker. The runtime spill anchor (the formula cell at the top-left of a spilled range) lives in `Workbook::spill_anchors` (§ 7), not in the AST. Phase 4.7 does not construct `Expr::Spill`; that's Phase 4.9's job alongside `@`.

## 5. Binder — `ExprPlan::Array`

New variant:

```rust
ExprPlan::Array(Vec<Vec<ExprPlan>>)
```

Binder rule: `Expr::Array` → `ExprPlan::Array` element-wise. Each element binds to a `Number` / `Bool` / `String` / `Error` plan (no `Unary` — the lexer folds signs into literals).

**Codex MEDIUM:** `Expr::Array` is a PUBLIC AST variant. Tests + tools can construct invalid ASTs (e.g. ragged shapes via `Vec::push`). The binder MUST reject ragged ASTs with a clean error, not panic:

```rust
BindError::ArrayRowArityMismatch { expected: u32, found: u32, row: u32 }
```

This shadows the equivalent `ParseError` for the case where the AST was constructed directly. No `BindError::UnsupportedVariant` for `Expr::Array` itself — it's now supported.

/bin/zsh -lc "sed -n '220,238p' docs/architecture/2026-05-14-array-formulas-and-spills.md" in /Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine
 succeeded in 0ms:
`EvalResult` (still in `ql-exec`) wraps the ABI-side return at the eval boundary. No cycle.

### 6.3 Eval contexts — REDESIGN per Codex HIGH

The original "scalar context returns first cell" rule was **NOT Excel canon**. Excel's behavior:
- **Dynamic-array Excel (365+):** array-in-scalar-context spills. `@` explicitly opts back into legacy implicit-intersection.
- **Legacy Excel:** implicit intersection takes the cell at the row/col INTERSECTION of the calling formula's row/col with the array's row/col span. "First cell" is wrong in both modes.

**Revised v1 rule:** array-in-scalar-context produces `Value::Error(ErrorValue::Calc)`. Specifically:

- **Cell-boundary context** (the formula expression at a cell's root): array result → spill (§ 8).
- **Sub-expression scalar context** (binary operand, scalar function arg): array result → `#CALC!`.
- **Aggregate-arg context** (existing `BindContext::AggregateArg` from Phase 2B.4): array result is consumed as a 2D range of values, NOT as a single scalar. This already works for `RangeRef` and named ranges; extending to literal arrays just feeds the cells.

Test:
- `=SEQUENCE(3) + 1` (no spill — wrapped in `+1` binary op) → `#CALC!`. The `+1` operand context is scalar.
- `=SUM(SEQUENCE(3))` → 6. The SUM arg context is aggregate; SEQUENCE's 3×1 result feeds as 3 cells.
- `=SEQUENCE(3)` at cell A1 → spills to A1, A2, A3.

**Section Verdicts**

1. PASS - Acceptance criteria are the right Phase 4.7 north star.

2. FLAG - LOW: Current-state inventory is stale: `ErrorValue::Spill` and qbook error sigil round-trip already exist in HEAD. Fix by deleting §9.3 as new work and moving it to “already in place.”

3. FLAG - MEDIUM: Constant-only array literals are a reasonable v1 cut, but the doc overclaims Excel compatibility around arity padding and omits error constants. Fix by explicitly documenting this as a Quantbook v1 subset and consider allowing error literals like `#N/A`.

4. FLAG - LOW: `Expr::Spill(Box<Expr>)` is described as a runtime anchor, but Excel’s syntactic spill operator is the spill-range ref `A1#`, not the anchor. Fix the terminology and reserve `Expr::Spill` for future spill-ref syntax, or rename the planned runtime concept.

5. FLAG - MEDIUM: Binder “panic if arity violated” is too brittle because `Expr::Array` is public and tests/tools can construct invalid ASTs. Fix by returning a bind/validation error instead of panicking.

6. FLAG - HIGH: The evaluator/function-boundary design is wrong in two ways: `ql-functions` cannot register an `ArrayFn` over `ql_exec::EvalResult` without a crate dependency cycle, and “scalar context returns first cell” is not Excel canon as a default rule. Fix by defining the array-capable function ABI in a dependency-neutral crate or unifying `FnArg` in `ql-functions`, and model scalarization as explicit implicit-intersection semantics, not unconditional first-cell truncation. Microsoft documents implicit intersection as context/position based for ranges and `@`-driven in dynamic Excel, not a blanket first-cell rule: https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/office/implicit-intersection-operator-ce3be07b-0101-4450-a24e-c1c999be2b34

7. FLAG - MEDIUM: `SpillAnchorTable` map invariants are fine, but low-level mutators are underspecified because `unregister_spill` is later expected to clear computed overlays even though the table cannot do that alone. Fix by making the table map-only and putting overlay clearing in `Workbook`/runtime APIs that own storage access.

8. FLAG - HIGH: Runtime writeback misses the mandatory “clear old spill before every re-eval” path and does not define how to avoid the old spill blocking itself. Fix by making spill writeback always unregister and clear the anchor’s prior spill footprint before evaluating/registering the new result, then restore `#SPILL!` at anchor only if the new target is blocked.

9. FLAG - MEDIUM: Blocking semantics need tightening: the example `{1,2;3,4}` blocked by `D1` is wrong, degenerate arrays should be a function/eval error contract rather than left to implementation time, and formulas/computed non-spill cells must be treated as occupied. Fix tests and specify exact error precedence.

10. FLAG - HIGH: Option A as written does not hold under the current graph model. `outgoing` means “formula depends on producer,” dirty propagation walks reverse indices, and `CalcgraphSession` currently treats `CellNode`s as formula nodes for scheduling; eager spill-target `CellNode`s plus non-Tarjan adjacency will either lose ordering or schedule non-formula targets. Fix by modeling spill targets as producer aliases: formulas reading a spill target should depend on the anchor formula node, and anchor changes should dirty readers of every target; do not make spill targets ordinary formula nodes.

11. FLAG - MEDIUM: The sub-phase order ships runtime invalidation before the calcgraph model is settled, which risks building against the wrong dependency surface. Move graph/spill-source design earlier, before runtime invalidation and writeback hardening.

12. FLAG - HIGH: The “no schema bump because computed overlay round-trips” claim is false for spill targets. Current qbook save emits non-formula nonblank cells as ordinary records, and load routes non-formula records to the user overlay; persisted spill targets would reload as blockers and turn the anchor into `#SPILL!`. Fix by either skipping derived spill-target cells during save and recomputing them on load, or bumping schema to persist value lane / spill metadata explicitly.

13. FLAG - MEDIUM: First-wave functions are the right set, but their error/shape contracts are underdefined, especially `SEQUENCE(0, ...)`, `FILTER` empty results, and horizontal vs vertical mask matching. Fix before implementation with exact Excel-compatible argument validation and output orientation rules.

14. FLAG - HIGH: The known-unknowns contain two decisions that should not proceed: eager target `CellNode` creation is expensive and semantically wrong for the current scheduler, and a fourth function tier is the trigger to unify dispatch rather than continue tier proliferation. Fix by using lazy producer-alias metadata for spills and designing one array-capable registry ABI.

15. PASS - The out-of-scope list is sensible for a v1 dynamic-array slice.

16. FLAG - HIGH: The open questions include unresolved architecture decisions that block 4.7.A if left open, especially graph aliasing, function ABI, and scalarization semantics. Fix by closing those in the design doc before lexer/parser work starts.

**Answers To Section 16**

1. Option A does not hold as written. A separate non-cycle adjacency can be useful, but it must not be “spill target cell node behaves like a dirty formula node”; the scheduler needs direct formula dependency edges or supplemental edges from readers to the spill anchor.

2. Do not add `ArrayFn` as a fourth parallel tier. Unify around an enum/trait function ABI that can carry scalar, range, array, and context, or extend `FnArg`/`RangeAwareFn` into an array-aware `FunctionArg` plus `FunctionContext`.

3. Prefer lazy metadata, not eager `CellNode` creation. Create graph structure only when a formula actually depends on a spill target, and keep spill target occupancy in storage/runtime maps.

4. No, the binder + dirty propagator are not currently strong enough. `ExprPlan::CellRef` to a spill target reads storage, but dependency extraction has no way to know that the true producer is the spill anchor; add a `cell_to_spill_anchor` producer lookup during dependency extraction or supplemental scheduling.

5. Restricting array literal cells to constants is acceptable for v1, but include error constants or explicitly defer them. Refs/functions inside array constants can wait until the array evaluator and dependency extraction are stable.

**What Design Decisions Are Still Wrong**

HIGH:
- Default “scalar context returns first cell” is the wrong semantic model; implement explicit implicit intersection / `@` behavior later instead of silently truncating arrays.
- `ArrayFn` over `ql_exec::EvalResult` crosses crate boundaries incorrectly and should be replaced with a unified array-capable function ABI.
- Spill graph modeling via eager target `CellNode`s and parallel adjacency is incompatible with current formula-node scheduling.
- “No schema bump” is unsafe unless save skips spill-derived target cells; otherwise reload converts spill outputs into user blockers.
- Old spill footprints must be cleared before every spill re-evaluation, including blocked re-evaluation.

MEDIUM:
- `SpillAnchorTable` should be map-only; overlay clearing belongs in workbook/runtime APIs.
- Function specs need exact error precedence and shape validation before implementation.
- Binder should reject invalid public AST shapes with errors, not panics.
- Sub-phase order should settle graph/function ABI before runtime work.

LOW:
- Current-state text is stale for `ErrorValue::Spill`.
- Spill terminology should distinguish anchor, target, and `A1#` spill-ref syntax.
- Fix the invalid blocking example using `D1` for a 2x2 spill.

     1	//! `ExprPlan` — execution-ready intermediate representation.
     2	//!
     3	//! Sits between `ql_formula_syntax::Expr` (parser output, may carry `Option<SheetId>` for
     4	//! unresolved same-sheet refs) and the kernel dispatch in `scalar.rs` / `simd.rs` (W4-2).
     5	//! `bind()` walks the Expr tree and resolves every `CellAddr.sheet: Option<SheetId>` to a
     6	//! concrete `SheetId` based on the formula's owning sheet.
     7	//!
     8	//! The plan is "execution-ready" but still tree-shaped — it does NOT yet flatten the
     9	//! expression into a SIMD-friendly opcode stream. That's the role of `kernel_simd` in W4-2,
    10	//! which lowers `ExprPlan::Binary { op: Mul, lhs: CellRef, rhs: Number }` into a
    11	//! `multiversion`-dispatched `Float64Array * f64` call.
    12	//!
    13	//! ## Phase 0 scope
    14	//!
    15	//! Subset of `Expr` variants covered:
    16	//! - `Number(f64)` — numeric literal
    17	//! - `Bool(bool)` — boolean literal
    18	//! - `String(Arc<str>)` — text literal (binds 1:1)
    19	//! - `CellRef { sheet, row, col, abs_col, abs_row }` — sheet RESOLVED to concrete SheetId
    20	//! - `Binary { op, lhs, rhs }` — arithmetic + concat + comparison
    21	//! - `Unary { op, operand }` — unary minus / plus / percent
    22	//!
    23	//! Deferred (will surface in W4-4 ql-functions integration):
    24	//! - `RangeRef` — single range used inside a Function call (e.g. `SUM(A:A)`)
    25	//! - `Function { name, args }` — registry dispatch
    26	//! - `Array`, `Spill` — Phase 3+ dynamic arrays

    30	use ql_formula_syntax::{Expr, Operator, SheetRef};
    31	use ql_types::{ColId, ErrorValue, Range, RowId, SheetId};

    33	/// Bound, execution-ready expression. Sheet refs are concrete.
    34	#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]
    35	pub enum ExprPlan {
    36	    Number(f64),
    37	    Bool(bool),
    38	    String(Arc<str>),
    39	    CellRef {
    40	        sheet: SheetId,
    41	        row: RowId,
    42	        col: ColId,
    43	        abs_col: bool,
    44	        abs_row: bool,
    45	    },
    46	    Binary {
    47	        op: Operator,
    48	        lhs: Box<ExprPlan>,
    49	        rhs: Box<ExprPlan>,
    50	    },
    51	    Unary {
    52	        op: Operator,
    53	        operand: Box<ExprPlan>,
    54	    },
    55	    /// Function call. Args are bound recursively. Phase 0 W4-5: name is preserved
    56	    /// verbatim from the AST (Excel-canonical uppercase normalization happens at the
    57	    /// parser level — W4-5 expects already-uppercased names from the binder). The
    58	    /// scalar evaluator looks up the name in a `FunctionRegistry` at evaluation time.
    59	    Function {
    60	        name: Arc<str>,
    61	        args: Vec<ExprPlan>,
    62	    },
    63	    /// Phase 2B.4 (2026-05-12): a `NameRef` resolving to a `NamedTarget::Range`
    64	    /// in an aggregate-argument position (e.g. the arg of `SUM`, `AVERAGE`,
    65	    /// `COUNT`). Carries the canonical name + the resolved Range. The scalar
    66	    /// evaluator currently rejects this with `Value::Error(ErrorValue::Calc)`
    67	    /// because aggregate-range evaluation is Engine Phase 3.6 work; the bind
    68	    /// shape lands now so the IDE / op-log surfaces are stable when Phase 3.6
    69	    /// flips the eval on.
    70	    ///
    71	    /// In scalar (non-aggregate) positions, the same `NameRef` produces
    72	    /// [`BindError::NamedRangeInScalarContext`] instead of binding to this
    73	    /// variant.
    74	    AggregateNameRef {
    75	        /// Canonical (uppercase) name as it appears in the NameTable.
    76	        name: Arc<str>,
    77	        /// Pre-resolved range payload. Phase 3.6 evaluator reads from this
    78	        /// directly rather than re-looking-up the name.
    79	        range: Range,
    80	    },
    81	
    82	    /// **W5-99 (Phase 4.7.F):** array literal lowered from `Expr::Array`.
    83	    /// Row-major; outer `Vec` is rows, inner `Vec` is cells. Element-arity
    84	    /// is invariant — every row has the same length. The binder enforces
    85	    /// this (`BindError::ArrayRowArityMismatch`) on every public input AND
    86	    /// the parser already enforces it (`ParseError::ArrayRowArityMismatch`);
    87	    /// defense in depth — the binder can't trust the AST because
    88	    /// `Expr::Array` is a public variant that tests / tools can construct
    89	    /// directly with ragged shapes.
    90	    ///
    91	    /// Inner `ExprPlan`s are restricted to the array-cell subset per
    92	    /// design § 3.3: `Number` / `Bool` / `String` / `Error`. Cell-refs,
    93	    /// function calls, and nested arrays are NOT permitted in v1 (already
    94	    /// rejected at parse time via `ParseError::InvalidArrayCellToken`).
    95	    /// The binder validates the same subset as defense in depth.
    96	    ///

    98	    /// an `ArrayValue` from this plan and routes to either the cell-
    99	    /// boundary spill path (cell context) or `#CALC!` (scalar context),
   100	    /// per design § 6.3.
   101	    Array(Vec<Vec<ExprPlan>>),

   104	    /// `Expr::Error(ev)`. The evaluator returns `Value::Error(ev)` directly;
   105	    /// the lowering is mechanical (no resolution, no context). Used by
   106	    /// `=#REF!` formulas, `IFERROR(..., #N/A)` fallback args, and error
   107	    /// literals inside `Expr::Array` cells.
   108	    Error(ErrorValue),
   109	}
   110	
   111	/// Error during binding. Phase 2A.1 added `UnresolvedName`; Phase 2A.6 added
   112	/// `NamedTargetIs{Blank,Error}`; Phase 2A.11 audit M16 switched to
   113	/// `thiserror::Error` for consistency with the rest of the error surface.
   114	/// Display strings are now user-facing (suitable for IDE diagnostics).
   115	#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, thiserror::Error)]
   116	pub enum BindError {
   117	    /// The Expr contains a variant not supported in this build (e.g. RangeRef
   118	    /// outside a Function context, Array literal, Spill anchor, NamedTarget::Range
   119	    /// — all Engine Phase 4 work per `docs/MASTER-PLAN.md`).
   120	    #[error("unsupported expression variant: {0}")]
   121	    UnsupportedVariant(&'static str),
   122	    /// A `NameRef` was used but the name isn't registered in the active `NameTable`.
   123	    /// Phase 2A.1: equivalent to Excel's `#NAME?` but surfaced at bind time rather
   124	    /// than as a runtime Error value, so the IDE can highlight the offending token
   125	    /// before evaluation.
   126	    #[error("unresolved name {0:?}")]
   127	    UnresolvedName(Arc<str>),
   128	    /// A `NameRef` resolved to a `NamedTarget::Constant(Value::Blank)`. Phase 2A.6
   129	    /// audit M2: previously this was silently coerced to `Text("")`, producing
   130	    /// nonsense in arithmetic contexts. Now surfaced loudly so callers fix the
   131	    /// data binding.
   132	    #[error("named constant {0:?} is blank; cannot be used in this context")]
   133	    NamedTargetIsBlank(Arc<str>),
   134	    /// A `NameRef` resolved to a `NamedTarget::Constant(Value::Error(_))`. Phase 2A.6
   135	    /// audit M3: previously silently mapped to `UnresolvedName` (wrong error class —
   136	    /// the name IS resolved, just to an error value). Carries the underlying
   137	    /// `ErrorValue` so the IDE can echo `#DIV/0!` / `#REF!` etc. accurately.
   138	    // Phase 2A.13 audit cycle-3 M1: `{1}` (Display) instead of `{1:?}` (Debug).
   139	    // `ErrorValue` has a `Display` impl that renders the canonical Excel sigil
   140	    // (`#DIV/0!` etc.); the prior debug formatter leaked Rust enum identifiers
   141	    // ("DivZero") into IDE diagnostics — the same mistake WS-4 was supposed to
   142	    // close everywhere. Note `{0:?}` is kept deliberately on the name field
   143	    // so the Arc<str> is rendered with surrounding quotes for visual clarity
   144	    // ("named constant \"X\" ..." vs the bare-string ambiguous "named constant X").
   145	    #[error("named constant {0:?} holds an error value: {1}")]
   146	    NamedTargetIsError(Arc<str>, ErrorValue),
   147	    /// Phase 2B.4 (2026-05-12): a `NameRef` resolving to a `NamedTarget::Range`
   148	    /// appeared in a scalar-context position (e.g. `=Sales + 1` instead of
   149	    /// `=SUM(Sales)`). Excel returns `#VALUE!` at evaluation; we surface it at
   150	    /// bind so the IDE highlights the offending token before any state writes.
   151	    /// Distinct from the generic `UnsupportedVariant` so callers can render a
   152	    /// specific "this name is a range; use it inside an aggregate function"
   153	    /// hint.
   154	    #[error(
   155	        "named range {0:?} cannot be used in this scalar position; \
   156	         wrap it in an aggregate function such as SUM, AVERAGE, COUNT, MIN, MAX"
   157	    )]
   158	    NamedRangeInScalarContext(Arc<str>),
   159	    /// Phase 2B.4 (2026-05-12): a `NameRef` resolving to a
   160	    /// `NamedTarget::Formula` in a scalar-context position. Named formulas
   161	    /// (`Profit = Revenue - Costs`) are Engine Phase 4 work; this distinct
   162	    /// variant tells the IDE / caller that the name is recognized but the
   163	    /// named-formula feature itself is not yet shipped.
   164	    #[error(
   165	        "named formula {0:?} cannot be used in this position; \
   166	         named-formula resolution is Engine Phase 4 work"
   167	    )]
   168	    NamedFormulaUnsupported(Arc<str>),
   169	
   170	    /// **W5-90 (Phase 4.6.B):** `SheetRef::Name(N)` from cross-sheet
   171	    /// syntax (`Sheet1!A1`) didn't resolve to any sheet in the
   172	    /// workbook at bind time. Surfaces as a clean bind-time diagnostic
   173	    /// before any runtime read; closes XS-4-01.
   174	    #[error("unknown sheet {0:?}")]
   175	    UnknownSheet(Arc<str>),
   176	
   177	    /// **W5-99 (Phase 4.7.F):** `Expr::Array` row N has a different
   178	    /// length than row 0. The parser already enforces uniform arity
   179	    /// (`ParseError::ArrayRowArityMismatch`), but `Expr::Array` is a
   180	    /// PUBLIC variant — tests and tools can construct ragged arrays
   181	    /// directly. Defense-in-depth: the binder rejects ragged shapes
   182	    /// with this error rather than panicking (Codex MEDIUM-5 in the
   183	    /// W5-94 design review).
   184	    #[error("array literal row {row} has {found} cell(s), expected {expected} (matching row 0)")]
   185	    ArrayRowArityMismatch { expected: u32, found: u32, row: u32 },
   186	
   187	    /// **W5-99 (Phase 4.7.F):** `Expr::Array` contains a cell that
   188	    /// isn't in the bind-allowed subset (Number / Bool / String /
   189	    /// Error). The parser already restricts cell tokens at parse
   190	    /// time, but defense-in-depth applies to tests / tools that
   191	    /// construct `Expr::Array` directly. Lists what was found.
   192	    #[error("array literal contains a non-literal cell at row {row}, col {col}: {got}")]
   193	    ArrayCellNotLiteral {
   194	        row: u32,
   195	        col: u32,
   196	        got: &'static str,
   197	    },
   198	}
   199	
   200	/// Phase 2B.4 (2026-05-12): bind-time context for a sub-expression. Drives
   201	/// per-name-ref decisions about whether a `NamedTarget::Range` is acceptable
   202	/// (aggregate-arg) or surfaces as `BindError::NamedRangeInScalarContext`
   203	/// (scalar). Internal to the binder; not exposed in the public API yet.
   204	#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
   205	enum BindContext {
   206	    /// Default. NameRefs resolving to ranges or formulas are rejected with
   207	    /// precise errors.
   208	    Scalar,
   209	    /// Inside the argument list of an aggregate function (SUM, AVERAGE,

   214	}

   245	    // parallel `range_aware_fns` table. But the binder uses this list to

   248	    // here so the binder allows the AggregateNameRef in arg position. The
   249	    // eval-side dispatch routes correctly via `lookup_range_aware` first.
   250	    matches!(
   251	        name,
   252	        "SUM"
   253	            | "AVERAGE"
   254	            | "AVG"
   255	            | "COUNT"
   256	            | "COUNTA"
   257	            | "MIN"
   258	            | "MAX"
   259	            | "PRODUCT"
   260	            | "VAR"

     1	//! Scalar evaluator — `eval_scalar(plan, env) -> Value`.
     2	//!
     3	//! Per-cell evaluation path. Used for:
     4	//! - Single-cell formulas (`=A1+B1` in cell C1).
     5	//! - Per-cell fallback inside a FormulaRegion when SIMD isn't applicable (text, error,
     6	//!   mixed types, non-arithmetic ops, division — see lower.rs H5 routing).
     7	//! - Tests, which is most of the W4-1 coverage.
     8	//!
     9	//! The SIMD hot path for OG-02 lives in `simd.rs` and bypasses this entirely —
    10	//! it consumes Arrow Float64Array slices directly via `multiversion`-dispatched
    11	//! kernels.
    12	//!
    13	//! ## Excel binary arithmetic semantics (Phase 2A.9 audit M1 — lenient coercion)
    14	//!
    15	//! For binary operators `+ - * / ^`:
    16	//! - If either operand is an `Error`, propagate that error (left operand wins).
    17	//! - Coerce both operands to f64 via `to_number_lenient` (Number → self;
    18	//!   Bool → 0/1; Blank → 0; Text that parses as a number → that number; Text
    19	//!   that doesn't parse → `#VALUE!`; Error → propagate). Phase 2A.9 audit M1
    20	//!   replaced the prior strict path that rejected ALL text.
    21	//! - Apply the f64 op. Sanitize result via `sanitize_f64` (NaN/Inf → `#NUM!`).
    22	//! - Special cases (Phase 2A.9 audit M3 / 2A.13 audit M3 companion):
    23	//!   - Division by zero → `#DIV/0!`.
    24	//!   - Negative base ^ non-integer exponent → `#NUM!`.
    25	//!   - `0^0` → `#NUM!`.
    26	//!   - `0^-n` (n > 0) → `#DIV/0!` (mathematically `1/0^n`).
    27	//!
    28	//! For `&` (concat): coerce both to text via `to_text_for_formula`; concat. Errors
    29	//! propagate.
    30	//!
    31	//! Comparison operators (`=`, `<>`, `<`, `>`, `<=`, `>=`) follow Excel-canon
    32	//! cross-type ordering (Phase 2A.9 audit M2): same-type pairs compare per
    33	//! their natural ordering; mixed-type pairs use type rank
    34	//! (`Number < Text < Boolean`). `Blank` coerces to `0` vs Number and `""`
    35	//! vs Text; for `Blank vs Boolean` the type-rank fallthrough applies (Blank
    36	//! ranks as Number, so `Blank < TRUE`). See `compare_values_excel`.
    37	//!
    38	//! Unary `-x` negates; `+x` no-ops; `x%` divides by 100.
    39	
    40	use ql_formula_syntax::Operator;
    41	use ql_functions::FunctionRegistry;
    42	use ql_types::{coercion, ErrorValue, Value};
    43	
    44	use crate::aggregate_cache::{AggregateCache, NoAggregateCache};
    45	use crate::env::CellEnv;
    46	use crate::plan::ExprPlan;
    47	
    48	/// Evaluate an `ExprPlan` against `env` to produce a `Value`. Total function — never
    49	/// panics on Excel semantics (NaN, div-by-zero, etc.). Panics only on internal
    50	/// programmer errors (e.g. malformed ExprPlan that bind() couldn't produce).
    51	///
    52	/// `Function` variants return `#NAME?` (`ErrorValue::Name`). To dispatch through a
    53	/// registry, use `eval_scalar_with_registry` instead.
    54	pub fn eval_scalar<E: CellEnv>(plan: &ExprPlan, env: &E) -> Value {
    55	    match plan {
    56	        ExprPlan::Number(n) => Value::number(*n),
    57	        ExprPlan::Bool(b) => Value::Boolean(*b),
    58	        ExprPlan::String(s) => Value::Text(s.clone()),
    59	        ExprPlan::CellRef {
    60	            sheet, row, col, ..
    61	        } => env.read_cell(*sheet, *row, *col),
    62	        ExprPlan::Binary { op, lhs, rhs } => {
    63	            let l = eval_scalar(lhs, env);
    64	            let r = eval_scalar(rhs, env);
    65	            eval_binary(*op, l, r)
    66	        }
    67	        ExprPlan::Unary { op, operand } => {
    68	            let v = eval_scalar(operand, env);
    69	            eval_unary(*op, v)
    70	        }
    71	        ExprPlan::Function { .. } => Value::Error(ErrorValue::Name),
    72	        // **Legacy no-registry path only.** Phase 3.6 / W5-39 added
    73	        // real aggregate-over-named-range evaluation, but ONLY in
    74	        // `eval_scalar_with_cache` (the cache-enabled entry point).
    75	        // This older `eval_scalar` function is kept for tests + the
    76	        // SIMD per-cell fallback path; it has no registry handle
    77	        // and intentionally surfaces `#CALC!` for any aggregate-
    78	        // named-range reference. Callers that want actual aggregate
    79	        // semantics must go through `eval_scalar_with_registry` or
    80	        // `eval_scalar_with_cache`.
    81	        ExprPlan::AggregateNameRef { .. } => Value::Error(ErrorValue::Calc),
    82	        // **W5-99 (Phase 4.7.F):** error literal — return the error
    83	        // value directly. Used by `=#REF!` formulas and error literals
    84	        // inside array cells.
    85	        ExprPlan::Error(ev) => Value::Error(*ev),
    86	        // **W5-99 (Phase 4.7.F):** array literal in scalar context.
    87	        // Per design § 6.3 (Codex HIGH-2 fix): array-in-scalar-context
    88	        // surfaces as `#CALC!`. This applies to the cell-boundary
    89	        // ALSO until the runtime spill path lands in W5-102 / 4.7.J;
    90	        // for now, even array literals at the cell root produce
    91	        // `#CALC!`. The spill path will route around this by detecting
    92	        // `ExprPlan::Array` at the cell-boundary BEFORE calling
    93	        // `eval_scalar_*` and materializing an `ArrayValue` instead.
    94	        ExprPlan::Array(_) => Value::Error(ErrorValue::Calc),
    95	    }
    96	}
    97	
    98	/// Like `eval_scalar` but dispatches `Function` variants through a `FunctionRegistry`.
    99	/// Unknown function names return `#NAME?`. Args are evaluated left-to-right with this
   100	/// same function (recursive); error-args propagate naturally through the registered
   101	/// function's logic.
   102	pub fn eval_scalar_with_registry<E: CellEnv>(
   103	    plan: &ExprPlan,
   104	    env: &E,
   105	    registry: &FunctionRegistry,
   106	) -> Value {
   107	    // Phase 3.6: legacy entry point — no aggregate cache. Wraps the cached
   108	    // path with `NoAggregateCache` (zero-cost no-op impl). Callers that
   109	    // have a session-owned cache should call `eval_scalar_with_cache`.
   110	    eval_scalar_with_cache(plan, env, registry, &NoAggregateCache)
   111	}
   112	
   113	/// **Phase 3.6 (2026-05-12) — W5-39, AGG-3-01..04 entry point.** Like
   114	/// `eval_scalar_with_registry` but consults `cache` for aggregate-over-
   115	/// range evaluations. When the Function branch detects an aggregate fn
   116	/// with an `AggregateNameRef` arg, it:
   117	///
   118	/// 1. Looks up `(range, function_name)` in `cache`. Hit → returns the
   119	///    cached value without re-scanning the range.
   120	/// 2. Miss → materializes the range via `CellEnv::read_range`, calls
   121	///    the aggregator, stores the result in `cache`, returns it.
   122	///
   123	/// The cache is invalidated externally (via `CalcgraphSession::
   124	/// mark_dirty_from_cell_write` → `InMemAggregateCache::invalidate_at`)
   125	/// when a cell inside any cached range is written. The evaluator itself
   126	/// never invalidates — it only reads + stores.
   127	///
   128	/// Pass `&NoAggregateCache` for the legacy "always recompute" behavior.
   129	pub fn eval_scalar_with_cache<E: CellEnv>(
   130	    plan: &ExprPlan,
   131	    env: &E,
   132	    registry: &FunctionRegistry,
   133	    cache: &dyn AggregateCache,
   134	) -> Value {
   135	    match plan {
   136	        ExprPlan::Number(n) => Value::number(*n),
   137	        ExprPlan::Bool(b) => Value::Boolean(*b),
   138	        ExprPlan::String(s) => Value::Text(s.clone()),
   139	        ExprPlan::CellRef {
   140	            sheet, row, col, ..
   141	        } => env.read_cell(*sheet, *row, *col),
   142	        ExprPlan::Binary { op, lhs, rhs } => {
   143	            let l = eval_scalar_with_cache(lhs, env, registry, cache);
   144	            let r = eval_scalar_with_cache(rhs, env, registry, cache);
   145	            eval_binary(*op, l, r)
   146	        }
   147	        ExprPlan::Unary { op, operand } => {
   148	            let v = eval_scalar_with_cache(operand, env, registry, cache);
   149	            eval_unary(*op, v)
   150	        }
   151	        ExprPlan::Function { name, args } => {
   152	            // W5-53 (GAP-F-05 closure): check the range-aware table
   153	            // FIRST. Range-aware functions like SUMIF need per-arg
   154	            // range-vs-scalar metadata that the scalar `&[Value]`
   155	            // contract can't carry. If a function is registered as
   156	            // range-aware, build `Vec<FnArg>` with the correct
   157	            // variant per arg (`AggregateNameRef` → `Range(read_range)`,
   158	            // everything else → `Scalar(eval)`).
   159	            if let Some(raf) = registry.lookup_range_aware(name) {
   160	                use ql_functions::FnArg;
   161	                let mut fn_args: Vec<FnArg> = Vec::with_capacity(args.len());
   162	                for a in args {
   163	                    match a {
   164	                        ExprPlan::AggregateNameRef { range, .. } => {
   165	                            // W5-54: shape-aware Range so VLOOKUP /
   166	                            // HLOOKUP / INDEX can address by (row,
   167	                            // col). SUMIF / COUNTIF ignore shape.
   168	                            let (values, rows, cols) = env.read_range_with_shape(*range);
   169	                            fn_args.push(FnArg::Range { values, rows, cols });
   170	                        }
   171	                        other => {
   172	                            fn_args.push(FnArg::Scalar(eval_scalar_with_cache(
   173	                                other, env, registry, cache,
   174	                            )));
   175	                        }
   176	                    }
   177	                }
   178	                return raf(&fn_args);
   179	            }
   180	            // **W5-69 (Phase 4.5.A.0):** check the context-aware table
   181	            // SECOND. Date / locale / clock-aware functions (DATE, NOW,
   182	            // TODAY, WEEKDAY, ...) take an extra `&EvalContext` arg in
   183	            // addition to pre-evaluated `&[Value]`. Args are evaluated
   184	            // left-to-right exactly like the scalar path; only the
   185	            // function signature differs. EvalContext is sourced from
   186	            // `env.eval_context()` (default is Excel1900 + EnUs +
   187	            // System).
   188	            if let Some(caf) = registry.lookup_context_aware(name) {
   189	                let evaluated: Vec<Value> = args
   190	                    .iter()
   191	                    .map(|a| eval_scalar_with_cache(a, env, registry, cache))
   192	                    .collect();
   193	                return caf(&evaluated, env.eval_context());
   194	            }
   195	            match registry.lookup(name) {
   196	                Some(f) => {
   197	                    // Phase 3.6 (2026-05-12) — AGG-3-04 correctness + AGG-
   198	                    // 3-01 cache hit path. Detect aggregate-over-named-range
   199	                    // and route through the cache. The cache key is `(range,
   200	                    // function_name)` — the EXACT call shape, not just the
   201	                    // range — so `SUM(Sales)` and `AVERAGE(Sales)` are
   202	                    // separate entries.
   203	                    //
   204	                    // Single-AggregateNameRef-arg fast path: most aggregates
   205	                    // are called with a single named range (`=SUM(Sales)`).
   206	                    // We special-case that for cache use. Multi-range
   207	                    // aggregates (`SUM(Sales, Discount)`) fall back to no-
   208	                    // cache (compute on every call) — V1 limitation.
   209	                    let single_range_arg = match args.as_slice() {
   210	                        [ExprPlan::AggregateNameRef { range, .. }] => Some(*range),
   211	                        _ => None,
   212	                    };
   213	                    if let Some(range) = single_range_arg {
   214	                        if crate::plan::is_aggregate_function(name) {
   215	                            if let Some(cached) = cache.lookup_aggregate(range, name) {
   216	                                return cached;
   217	                            }
   218	                            let flat = env.read_range(range);
   219	                            let v = f(&flat);
   220	                            // Don't cache `Value::Error(_)` results — they
   221	                            // often signal env-level issues (missing sheet
   222	                            // → #REF!, empty range AVERAGE → #DIV/0!) that
   223	                            // a follow-up edit would resolve; better to
   224	                            // recompute than serve a stale error.
   225	                            if !matches!(v, Value::Error(_)) {
   226	                                cache.store_aggregate(range, name, v.clone());
   227	                            }
   228	                            return v;
   229	                        }
   230	                    }
   231	                    let has_range_arg = args
   232	                        .iter()
   233	                        .any(|a| matches!(a, ExprPlan::AggregateNameRef { .. }));
   234	                    if has_range_arg && crate::plan::is_aggregate_function(name) {
   235	                        // Multi-range or mixed aggregate args: materialize
   236	                        // every range and call the function. No cache use
   237	                        // (V2 may add multi-range cache keys).
   238	                        let mut flat: Vec<Value> = Vec::new();
   239	                        for a in args {
   240	                            match a {
   241	                                ExprPlan::AggregateNameRef { range, .. } => {
   242	                                    flat.extend(env.read_range(*range));
   243	                                }
   244	                                other => {
   245	                                    flat.push(eval_scalar_with_cache(other, env, registry, cache));
   246	                                }
   247	                            }
   248	                        }
   249	                        f(&flat)
   250	                    } else {
   251	                        let evaluated: Vec<Value> = args
   252	                            .iter()
   253	                            .map(|a| eval_scalar_with_cache(a, env, registry, cache))
   254	                            .collect();
   255	                        f(&evaluated)
   256	                    }
   257	                }
   258	                None => Value::Error(ErrorValue::Name),
   259	            }
   260	        }

     1	//! Engine Phase 3.1+3.2 (2026-05-12) — runtime ↔ calcgraph integration.
     2	//!
     3	//! `CalcgraphSession` owns the `ql_calcgraph::Graph` plus per-formula
     4	//! dependency tracking (cells / ranges / volatile / names). It lives
     5	//! at the engine level (in `ql-exec`) so it can reach
     6	//! `ql-storage::Workbook` for rebuild + `ql-formula-syntax` for
     7	//! lex/parse without dragging those deps into `ql-calcgraph`.
     8	//!
     9	//! ## Phase 3.1 (ownership shell, shipped W5-34)
    10	//!
    11	//! - Single-writer ownership: runtime borrows `&mut CalcgraphSession`
    12	//!   per edit (parallel to `&mut OpLog`).
    13	//! - O(1) `cell_index: HashMap<(SheetId, RowId, ColId), NodeId>` for
    14	//!   "which node represents this cell?" lookups.
    15	//! - 5 hooks (`on_set_{value,formula,name}`, `on_clear_formula`,
    16	//!   `on_add_sheet`) wired through `WorkbookRuntime`.
    17	//! - `rebuild_from_workbook` adds a `CellNode` per formula cell in
    18	//!   deterministic `(sheet, row, col)` sort order.
    19	//!
    20	//! ## Phase 3.2 (this commit) — dependency extraction
    21	//!
    22	//! - `walk_plan_for_deps` recursively walks a bound `ExprPlan`,
    23	//!   producing a `FormulaDeps` collection of (a) direct cell refs,
    24	//!   (b) named-range refs (from `AggregateNameRef`), (c) names
    25	//!   referenced (currently only via `AggregateNameRef`; see below),
    26	//!   (d) volatile-function presence.
    27	//! - `on_set_formula` now takes `&ExprPlan` and runs the extraction.
    28	//!   The session updates per-formula `formula_deps`, the volatile
    29	//!   set, and a name→formulas reverse index.
    30	//! - `rebuild_from_workbook` lex+parse+binds every formula and runs
    31	//!   the same extraction. Failures aggregate into
    32	//!   `RebuildResult { attempted, succeeded, failures }` — parallel
    33	//!   to `RecomputeResult` (Phase 2B.2). The session is still returned
    34	//!   on partial failure so the caller can inspect.
    35	//! - Dependency storage is **session-side**, not in the `Graph`. The
    36	//!   Phase 0 `Graph` edges are append-only; tracking the live deps on
    37	//!   the session lets Phase 3.3 implement re-registration cleanly
    38	//!   without needing edge removal (Phase 3.3 may swap in
    39	//!   Formualizer-style delta-edges for the graph too).
    40	//!
    41	//! ## Still deferred to Phase 3.3+
    42	//!
    43	//! - **Dirty propagation.** The 5 mutation hooks bump counters and
    44	//!   maintain dep state but don't yet fan out via
    45	//!   `Graph::dependents_for_cell`. Phase 3.3 wires that.
    46	//! - **Topological recompute.** `recompute_all` still HashMap-order
    47	//!   (GAP-R-01). Phase 3.4 replaces with Tarjan SCC.
    48	//! - **Name-dep tracking from non-AggregateNameRef paths.** When a
    49	//!   `NameRef` resolves to a Cell/Number/Bool/Text target, the binder
    50	//!   substitutes the underlying value and the name is lost. Today
    51	//!   such names invalidate via the PlanCache's `name_gen` counter
    52	//!   (Phase 2B.3) — a workbook-wide invalidation, not per-name.
    53	//!   Phase 3.3 may switch to per-name dirty propagation; until then
    54	//!   the PlanCache covers correctness.
    55	//!
    56	//! ## Ownership model
    57	//!
    58	//! ```text
    59	//!  IDE / caller
    60	//!    │
    61	//!    ├─ owns Workbook                      (storage)
    62	//!    ├─ owns OpLog                         (history; optional)
    63	//!    ├─ owns CalcgraphSession              (graph; optional, new in 3.1)
    64	//!    └─ owns FunctionRegistry              (process-wide singleton-ish)
    65	//!         │
    66	//!         └─ constructs WorkbookRuntime per edit (per-edit borrow window)
    67	//! ```
    68	//!
    69	//! Phase 6.1 `WorkbookSession` will absorb Workbook + OpLog +
    70	//! CalcgraphSession + PlanCache + FunctionRegistry into ONE owning
    71	//! struct that the binding crate can wrap cleanly (per GAP-PS-09).
    72	//! Today they're separate to keep refactor scope bounded.
    73	
    74	use std::collections::{HashMap, HashSet};
    75	use std::sync::Arc;
    76	
    77	use ql_calcgraph::{
    78	    range_contains_rowcol, schedule_with_supplemental, CellNode, Graph, Node, NodeId, Schedule,
    79	};
    80	use ql_formula_syntax::{lex, parse, RangeRef, SheetRef};
    81	use ql_storage::Workbook;
    82	use ql_types::{ColId, Range, RowId, SheetId};
    83

    88	/// Phase 3.3 (2026-05-12) — convert a `ql_types::Range` (used in
    89	/// `ExprPlan::AggregateNameRef`) to a `ql_formula_syntax::RangeRef`
    90	/// (used by `Graph::register_range_dependency`). The conversion picks
    91	/// the most precise variant available so the stripe index stays
    92	/// efficient:
    93	///
    94	/// - `start_row == 0 && end_row == RowId::MAX` → `WholeColumn` (one
    95	///   stripe per column in the range).
    96	/// - `start_col == 0 && end_col == ColId::MAX` → `WholeRow` (one
    97	///   stripe per row).
    98	/// - Otherwise → `Cells` (StripeIndex picks the smaller axis).
    99	///
   100	/// The Phase 0 `StripeIndex::register` panics on reversed bounds; this
   101	/// helper trusts `Range` to be normalized (per `Range::new` which
   102	/// sorts via `min/max`).
   103	fn range_to_rangeref(r: Range) -> RangeRef {
   104	    let whole_rows = r.start_row == 0 && r.end_row == RowId::MAX;
   105	    let whole_cols = r.start_col == 0 && r.end_col == ColId::MAX;
   106	    if whole_rows && !whole_cols {
   107	        RangeRef::WholeColumn {
   108	            sheet: SheetRef::Id(r.sheet),
   109	            start_col: r.start_col,
   110	            end_col: r.end_col,
   111	            abs_start: false,
   112	            abs_end: false,
   113	        }
   114	    } else if whole_cols && !whole_rows {
   115	        RangeRef::WholeRow {
   116	            sheet: SheetRef::Id(r.sheet),
   117	            start_row: r.start_row,
   118	            end_row: r.end_row,
   119	            abs_start: false,
   120	            abs_end: false,
   121	        }
   122	    } else {
   123	        // Bounded rectangle (or fully-saturated rectangle, which the
   124	        // stripe index handles by picking the smaller axis).
   125	        RangeRef::Cells {
   126	            sheet: SheetRef::Id(r.sheet),
   127	            start_col: r.start_col,
   128	            start_row: r.start_row,
   129	            end_col: r.end_col,
   130	            end_row: r.end_row,
   131	            abs_start_col: false,
   132	            abs_start_row: false,
   133	            abs_end_col: false,
   134	            abs_end_row: false,
   135	        }
   136	    }
   137	}
   138	
   139	/// Phase 3.2 (2026-05-12) — hardcoded list of functions whose RESULT
   140	/// depends on something other than their arguments (NOW changes every
   141	/// recompute even with no inputs; INDIRECT reads a cell named by a
   142	/// string at runtime). The Phase 3.7 volatile-invalidation pass will
   143	/// use this set to schedule volatile re-evaluation. Tracked here as
   144	/// V0; Engine Phase 4.3 (function library expansion) replaces with
   145	/// per-function metadata on `FunctionRegistry`.
   146	///
   147	/// Names MUST be canonical upper-case (matches the parser's
   148	/// canonicalization for `Expr::Function::name`).
   149	pub(crate) fn is_volatile_function(name: &str) -> bool {
   150	    matches!(
   151	        name,
   152	        // Time / date — value changes every recompute.
   153	        "NOW" | "TODAY"
   154	        // Pseudo-random — value changes every recompute.
   155	        | "RAND" | "RANDBETWEEN" | "RANDARRAY"
   156	        // Address-by-string — result depends on workbook structure;
   157	        // a cell rename anywhere can affect the result.
   158	        | "INDIRECT"
   159	        // Structural offset — result depends on the current grid.
   160	        | "OFFSET"
   161	        // Environment introspection.
   162	        | "INFO" | "CELL"
   163	    )
   164	}
   165	
   166	/// Phase 3.2 — direct-dep collection extracted from walking a bound
   167	/// `ExprPlan`. Fields are owned (no borrows) so the caller can pass
   168	/// the collection around or store it without lifetime ceremony.
   169	///
   170	/// `cells`, `named_ranges`, and `names` are deduplicated by the
   171	/// `extract_and_register_deps` orchestrator (the walker emits
   172	/// duplicates; the orchestrator collapses).
   173	#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default, PartialEq)]
   174	pub struct FormulaDeps {
   175	    /// Direct cell references (`ExprPlan::CellRef`). Resolved sheet
   176	    /// is included.
   177	    pub cells: Vec<(SheetId, RowId, ColId)>,
   178	    /// Named-range references (`ExprPlan::AggregateNameRef`). Each
   179	    /// entry pairs the canonical (uppercase) name with the resolved
   180	    /// `Range` payload.
   181	    pub named_ranges: Vec<(Arc<str>, Range)>,
   182	    /// Names referenced by the formula. Currently only names that
   183	    /// resolve to ranges populate this (via `AggregateNameRef`); see
   184	    /// module docs for the limitation around scalar-resolved names.
   185	    pub names: Vec<Arc<str>>,
   186	    /// `true` iff any volatile function appears anywhere in the
   187	    /// plan tree.
   188	    pub is_volatile: bool,
   189	}
   190	
   191	impl FormulaDeps {
   192	    /// Total dependency count (cells + named ranges). Useful for
   193	    /// summary metrics; Phase 3.10 megaudit / ql-profile will surface
   194	    /// per-formula dep counts in the graph-profile JSON.
   195	    pub fn len(&self) -> usize {
   196	        self.cells.len() + self.named_ranges.len()
   197	    }
   198	
   199	    pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
   200	        self.cells.is_empty() && self.named_ranges.is_empty()
   201	    }
   202	}
   203	
   204	/// Phase 3.2 — recursive walker. Accumulates dependencies + volatile-
   205	/// function presence by descending the `ExprPlan` tree. Does not
   206	/// allocate any nodes or touch any graph state; pure read.
   207	pub(crate) fn walk_plan_for_deps(plan: &ExprPlan, deps: &mut FormulaDeps) {
   208	    match plan {
   209	        ExprPlan::Number(_) | ExprPlan::Bool(_) | ExprPlan::String(_) => {
   210	            // Leaf literals carry no dependencies.
   211	        }
   212	        ExprPlan::CellRef {
   213	            sheet, row, col, ..
   214	        } => {
   215	            deps.cells.push((*sheet, *row, *col));
   216	        }
   217	        ExprPlan::Binary { lhs, rhs, .. } => {
   218	            walk_plan_for_deps(lhs, deps);
   219	            walk_plan_for_deps(rhs, deps);
   220	        }

   300	/// Resolve an Expr against an owning sheet, producing an ExprPlan. **No name
   301	/// resolution + no sheet resolution** — any `Expr::NameRef` produces
   302	/// `BindError::UnresolvedName`; any cross-sheet ref (`SheetRef::Name`)
   303	/// produces `BindError::UnknownSheet`.
   304	///
   305	/// `owning_sheet` is the sheet the formula lives on.
   306	pub fn bind(expr: &Expr, owning_sheet: SheetId) -> Result<ExprPlan, BindError> {
   307	    bind_with_names_and_sheets(expr, owning_sheet, &EmptyNameLookup, &EmptySheetResolver)
   308	}
   309	
   310	/// Phase 2A.1 — bind with a name resolver. **W5-90 update**: this entry
   311	/// point uses an `EmptySheetResolver`, so cross-sheet refs surface as
   312	/// `BindError::UnknownSheet`. Callers that need cross-sheet support
   313	/// should use [`bind_with_names_and_sheets`] and pass a real
   314	/// [`SheetResolver`] (typically `&Workbook` via the blanket impl).
   315	pub fn bind_with_names<L: NameLookup>(
   316	    expr: &Expr,
   317	    owning_sheet: SheetId,
   318	    names: &L,
   319	) -> Result<ExprPlan, BindError> {
   320	    bind_with_names_and_sheets(expr, owning_sheet, names, &EmptySheetResolver)
   321	}
   322	
   323	/// **W5-90 (Phase 4.6.B):** bind with BOTH name and sheet resolvers.
   324	/// Production callers (`WorkbookRuntime`, `WorkbookTransaction`,
   325	/// `CalcgraphSession`) pass `self.workbook.names()` for `names` and
   326	/// `self.workbook` for `sheets` (the blanket `SheetResolver for
   327	/// Workbook` impl in `env.rs` does the lookup via
   328	/// `Workbook::sheet_id_by_name`).
   329	pub fn bind_with_names_and_sheets<L: NameLookup>(
   330	    expr: &Expr,
   331	    owning_sheet: SheetId,
   332	    names: &L,
   333	    sheets: &dyn SheetResolver,
   334	) -> Result<ExprPlan, BindError> {
   335	    bind_with_context(expr, owning_sheet, names, sheets, BindContext::Scalar)
   336	}
   337	
   338	fn bind_with_context<L: NameLookup>(
   339	    expr: &Expr,
   340	    owning_sheet: SheetId,
   341	    names: &L,
   342	    sheets: &dyn SheetResolver,
   343	    ctx: BindContext,
   344	) -> Result<ExprPlan, BindError> {
   345	    match expr {
   346	        Expr::Number(n) => Ok(ExprPlan::Number(*n)),
   347	        Expr::Bool(b) => Ok(ExprPlan::Bool(*b)),
   348	        Expr::String(s) => Ok(ExprPlan::String(s.clone())),
   349	        Expr::CellRef(addr) => Ok(ExprPlan::CellRef {
   350	            sheet: resolve_sheet_ref(&addr.sheet, owning_sheet, sheets)?,
   351	            row: addr.row,
   352	            col: addr.col,
   353	            abs_col: addr.abs_col,
   354	            abs_row: addr.abs_row,
   355	        }),
   356	        Expr::Binary { op, lhs, rhs } => Ok(ExprPlan::Binary {
   357	            op: *op,
   358	            // Binary operands are always scalar context regardless of the
   359	            // outer context (Excel doesn't accept `=SUM(A1 + B1:B10)` —
   360	            // each operand to + is scalar).
   361	            lhs: Box::new(bind_with_context(
   362	                lhs,
   363	                owning_sheet,
   364	                names,
   365	                sheets,
   366	                BindContext::Scalar,
   367	            )?),
   368	            rhs: Box::new(bind_with_context(
   369	                rhs,
   370	                owning_sheet,
   371	                names,
   372	                sheets,
   373	                BindContext::Scalar,
   374	            )?),
   375	        }),
   376	        Expr::Unary { op, operand } => Ok(ExprPlan::Unary {
   377	            op: *op,
   378	            operand: Box::new(bind_with_context(
   379	                operand,
   380	                owning_sheet,
   381	                names,
   382	                sheets,
   383	                BindContext::Scalar,
   384	            )?),
   385	        }),
   386	        Expr::RangeRef(_) => Err(BindError::UnsupportedVariant(
   387	            "RangeRef requires a Function context; Phase 0 W4-1 has no function dispatch yet",
   388	        )),
   389	        Expr::Function { name, args } => {
   390	            // Phase 2B.4: switch context for aggregate-function arg lists.
   391	            let arg_ctx = if is_aggregate_function(name) {
   392	                BindContext::AggregateArg
   393	            } else {
   394	                BindContext::Scalar
   395	            };
   396	            let mut bound_args = Vec::with_capacity(args.len());
   397	            for a in args {
   398	                bound_args.push(bind_with_context(a, owning_sheet, names, sheets, arg_ctx)?);
   399	            }
   400	            Ok(ExprPlan::Function {
   401	                name: name.clone(),
   402	                args: bound_args,
   403	            })
   404	        }
   405	        // **W5-99 (Phase 4.7.F):** lower `Expr::Array` to
   406	        // `ExprPlan::Array`. Per design § 5, this is element-wise +
   407	        // enforces uniform row arity AND the restricted cell-kind subset
   408	        // (Number / Bool / String / Error). The parser already validates
   409	        // both; this is defense in depth against direct AST construction.
   410	        Expr::Array(rows) => {
   411	            if rows.is_empty() {
   412	                // The parser rejects `{}` with EmptyArrayLiteral, but a
   413	                // direct AST construction with `vec![]` could land here.
   414	                // No dedicated bind-error variant — surface as a clean
   415	                // UnsupportedVariant for the degenerate construction.
   416	                return Err(BindError::UnsupportedVariant(
   417	                    "Expr::Array with zero rows is not constructible from source; \
   418	                     direct AST construction with an empty Vec is rejected",
   419	                ));
   420	            }
   421	            let expected_arity = rows[0].len() as u32;
   422	            let mut bound_rows: Vec<Vec<ExprPlan>> = Vec::with_capacity(rows.len());
   423	            for (row_idx, row) in rows.iter().enumerate() {
   424	                let found_arity = row.len() as u32;
   425	                if found_arity != expected_arity {
   426	                    return Err(BindError::ArrayRowArityMismatch {
   427	                        expected: expected_arity,
   428	                        found: found_arity,
   429	                        row: row_idx as u32,
   430	                    });
   431	                }
   432	                let mut bound_row: Vec<ExprPlan> = Vec::with_capacity(row.len());
   433	                for (col_idx, cell) in row.iter().enumerate() {
   434	                    let bound_cell = match cell {
   435	                        Expr::Number(n) => ExprPlan::Number(*n),
   436	                        Expr::Bool(b) => ExprPlan::Bool(*b),
   437	                        Expr::String(s) => ExprPlan::String(s.clone()),
   438	                        Expr::Error(ev) => ExprPlan::Error(*ev),
   439	                        // Per design § 3.3 / parser restriction: only the
   440	                        // four literal kinds are allowed inside an array
   441	                        // cell. Reject everything else loudly so a future
   442	                        // test that constructs `Expr::Array(vec![vec![
   443	                        // Expr::CellRef(_)]])` directly surfaces a clean
   444	                        // error rather than silently producing a corrupt
   445	                        // ExprPlan.
   446	                        other => {
   447	                            return Err(BindError::ArrayCellNotLiteral {
   448	                                row: row_idx as u32,
   449	                                col: col_idx as u32,
   450	                                got: variant_kind(other),
   451	                            });
   452	                        }
   453	                    };
   454	                    bound_row.push(bound_cell);
   455	                }
   456	                bound_rows.push(bound_row);
   457	            }
   458	            Ok(ExprPlan::Array(bound_rows))
   459	        }
   460	
   461	        Expr::Spill(_) => Err(BindError::UnsupportedVariant(
   462	            "Spill-range-ref `A1#` syntax is Phase 4.9",
   463	        )),
   464	
   465	        // **W5-99 (Phase 4.7.F):** error literal — mechanical lowering.
   466	        // No resolution, no context. The evaluator returns
   467	        // `Value::Error(ev)` directly.
   468	        Expr::Error(ev) => Ok(ExprPlan::Error(*ev)),
   469	        Expr::NameRef(name) => {
   470	            // Phase 2A.6 audit M1: parser-emitted `NameRef` must always carry a
   471	            // non-empty canonical name. An empty name here means the parser is
   472	            // broken; panic to surface the upstream bug per the no-fallbacks rule.
   473	            assert!(
   474	                !name.is_empty(),
   475	                "bind_with_context: Expr::NameRef carries an empty string — parser invariant violated"
   476	            );
   477	            // Phase 2A.1: resolve the name via the active NameTable; Phase 2A.6
   478	            // audit H2 uses case-insensitive lookup in the `NameTable` impl so
   479	            // every entry point canonicalizes uniformly.
   480	            match names.lookup_named_target(name, owning_sheet) {
   481	                Some(ResolvedName::Cell(sheet, row, col, abs_col, abs_row)) => {
   482	                    Ok(ExprPlan::CellRef {
   483	                        sheet,
   484	                        row,
   485	                        col,
   486	                        abs_col,
   487	                        abs_row,
   488	                    })
   489	                }
   490	                Some(ResolvedName::Number(n)) => Ok(ExprPlan::Number(n)),
   491	                Some(ResolvedName::Bool(b)) => Ok(ExprPlan::Bool(b)),
   492	                Some(ResolvedName::Text(s)) => Ok(ExprPlan::String(s)),
   493	                // Phase 2B.4: context-aware Range handling.
   494	                Some(ResolvedName::Range(range)) => match ctx {
   495	                    BindContext::AggregateArg => Ok(ExprPlan::AggregateNameRef {
   496	                        name: name.clone(),
   497	                        range,
   498	                    }),
   499	                    BindContext::Scalar => Err(BindError::NamedRangeInScalarContext(name.clone())),
   500	                },
   501	                // Phase 2B.4: named formulas still unsupported (Engine Phase 4).
   502	                // We surface a distinct error rather than the generic
   503	                // UnsupportedVariant so the IDE can render a specific hint.
   504	                Some(ResolvedName::Formula(_)) => {
   505	                    Err(BindError::NamedFormulaUnsupported(name.clone()))
   506	                }
   507	                // Phase 2A.6 audit M2/M3: surface Blank- and Error-targets loudly.
   508	                Some(ResolvedName::Blank) => Err(BindError::NamedTargetIsBlank(name.clone())),
   509	                Some(ResolvedName::ErrorValue(e)) => {
   510	                    Err(BindError::NamedTargetIsError(name.clone(), e))
   511	                }
   512	                None => Err(BindError::UnresolvedName(name.clone())),
   513	            }
   514	        }
   515	    }
   516	}
   517	
   518	/// Variant describing a resolved name's target, mapped from `ql_storage::NamedTarget`.
   519	/// Phase 2A.1: this is the binder's intermediate vocabulary — the binder doesn't
   520	/// import ql-storage directly, so the lookup trait projects NamedTarget into this
   521	/// enum first. Phase 2 supports Cell + Constant; Range and Formula return
   522	/// unsupported errors.
   523	///
   524	/// Phase 2A.6 audit M2/M3: added `Blank` and `ErrorValue` so the binder can
   525	/// surface them as distinct `BindError` variants instead of either coercing
   526	/// silently or returning the wrong error class.
   527	#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]
   528	pub enum ResolvedName {
   529	    Cell(SheetId, RowId, ColId, bool, bool),
   530	    Number(f64),
   531	    Bool(bool),
   532	    Text(Arc<str>),
   533	    /// `NamedTarget::Range(_)` resolved with payload. Phase 2B.4 (2026-05-12):
   534	    /// previously carried no data; now threads the underlying `Range` so the
   535	    /// binder can pre-resolve into `ExprPlan::AggregateNameRef` without
   536	    /// asking the name table again at eval time.
   537	    Range(Range),
   538	    /// `NamedTarget::Formula(_)` resolved with payload. Phase 2B.4 added the
   539	    /// formula text payload; the binder rejects this for now (named-formula
   540	    /// resolution is Engine Phase 4) but the shape is in place.
   541	    Formula(Arc<str>),
   542	    /// `NamedTarget::Constant(Value::Blank)`. The binder rejects this loudly so
   543	    /// the caller can fix the data binding.
   544	    Blank,
   545	    /// `NamedTarget::Constant(Value::Error(...))`. The binder rejects with a
   546	    /// distinct error carrying the underlying `ErrorValue`.
   547	    ErrorValue(ErrorValue),
   548	}
   549	
   550	/// Name-resolution interface. `ql-exec` stays decoupled from `ql-storage`;
   551	/// the production caller wires a `Workbook` through this trait.
   552	///
   553	/// **W5-92 (Phase 4.6.D):** the trait now takes `owning_sheet` so impls
   554	/// that hold scoped tables (the `Workbook` blanket impl in
   555	/// `env.rs`) can run the two-tier chain: sheet-scoped first, then
   556	/// workbook-scoped. Impls without a sheet axis (legacy
   557	/// `NameLookup for NameTable`, `EmptyNameLookup`) ignore the parameter.
   558	pub trait NameLookup {
   559	    fn lookup_named_target(&self, name: &str, owning_sheet: SheetId) -> Option<ResolvedName>;
   560	}

   261	        // `AggregateNameRef` outside a Function context — the binder is
   262	        // supposed to surface `BindError::NamedRangeInScalarContext`

   264	        ExprPlan::AggregateNameRef { .. } => Value::Error(ErrorValue::Calc),

   271	        // path) lands in W5-102 / Phase 4.7.J; until then, every
   272	        // `ExprPlan::Array` evaluation produces `#CALC!`.
   273	        ExprPlan::Array(_) => Value::Error(ErrorValue::Calc),
