   276	
   277	fn eval_binary(op: Operator, lhs: Value, rhs: Value) -> Value {
   278	    // Error propagation comes first — Excel's left-error-wins rule.
   279	    if let Value::Error(e) = lhs {
   280	        return Value::Error(e);
   281	    }
   282	    if let Value::Error(e) = rhs {
   283	        return Value::Error(e);
   284	    }
   285	
   286	    match op {
   287	        Operator::Plus | Operator::Minus | Operator::Mul | Operator::Div | Operator::Pow => {
   288	            eval_arithmetic(op, &lhs, &rhs)
   289	        }
   290	        Operator::Concat => eval_concat(&lhs, &rhs),
   291	        Operator::Eq
   292	        | Operator::Neq
   293	        | Operator::Lt
   294	        | Operator::Le
   295	        | Operator::Gt
   296	        | Operator::Ge => eval_compare(op, &lhs, &rhs),
   297	        // Phase 0 doesn't produce Percent as a binary op (it's unary in Excel).
   298	        Operator::Percent => Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value),
   299	    }
   300	}
   301	
   302	fn eval_arithmetic(op: Operator, lhs: &Value, rhs: &Value) -> Value {
   303	    // Phase 2A.9 audit M1: arithmetic uses Excel-canonical *lenient* coercion.
   304	    // `"5" + 1` evaluates to `6` (text-that-parses-as-number coerces); only
   305	    // unparseable text returns `#VALUE!`. Phase 1 used the strict path which
   306	    // rejected ALL text — that was a documented Phase 0 deviation from Excel
   307	    // canon, with the lenient impl sitting unused in `ql-types::coercion`.
   308	    let lhs_num = match coercion::to_number_lenient(lhs) {
   309	        Ok(n) => n,
   310	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
   311	    };
   312	    let rhs_num = match coercion::to_number_lenient(rhs) {
   313	        Ok(n) => n,
   314	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
   315	    };
   316	
   317	    let result = match op {
   318	        Operator::Plus => lhs_num + rhs_num,
   319	        Operator::Minus => lhs_num - rhs_num,
   320	        Operator::Mul => lhs_num * rhs_num,
   321	        Operator::Div => {
   322	            if rhs_num == 0.0 {
   323	                return Value::Error(ErrorValue::DivZero);
   324	            }
   325	            lhs_num / rhs_num
   326	        }
   327	        Operator::Pow => {
   328	            // Excel: negative base with non-integer exponent → #NUM!. Plain f64::powf
   329	            // would produce NaN which sanitize_f64 would map to #NUM!, but check
   330	            // explicitly so the error type is clear.
   331	            if lhs_num < 0.0 && rhs_num.fract() != 0.0 {
   332	                return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Num);
   333	            }
   334	            // Phase 2A.13 audit cycle-3 M3 companion: `0^-n` is `#DIV/0!` per
   335	            // Excel canon (mathematically `0^-n = 1/0^n`). Rust/IEEE produces
   336	            // `+Inf`, which `sanitize_f64` would map to `#NUM!` — wrong error
   337	            // class. Guard before the `0^0` check (different precondition).
   338	            if lhs_num == 0.0 && rhs_num < 0.0 {
   339	                return Value::Error(ErrorValue::DivZero);
   340	            }
   341	            // Phase 2A.9 audit M3: Excel canon — `0^0` is `#NUM!`. Rust/IEEE
   342	            // produces 1.0, which would be a silent deviation. Guard explicitly.
   343	            if lhs_num == 0.0 && rhs_num == 0.0 {
   344	                return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Num);
   345	            }
   346	            lhs_num.powf(rhs_num)
   347	        }
   348	        _ => unreachable!("eval_arithmetic called with non-arithmetic op {op:?}"),
   349	    };
   350	
   351	    match coercion::sanitize_f64(result) {
   352	        Ok(n) => Value::Number(n),
   353	        Err(e) => Value::Error(e),
   354	    }
   355	}
   356	
   357	fn eval_concat(lhs: &Value, rhs: &Value) -> Value {
   358	    let l = match coercion::to_text_for_formula(lhs) {
   359	        Ok(s) => s,
   360	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
   361	    };
   362	    let r = match coercion::to_text_for_formula(rhs) {
   363	        Ok(s) => s,
   364	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
   365	    };
   366	    Value::text(format!("{l}{r}"))
   367	}
   368	
   369	fn eval_compare(op: Operator, lhs: &Value, rhs: &Value) -> Value {
   370	    // Phase 2A.9 audit M2: implement Excel-canonical cross-type comparison.
   371	    // Same-type pairs compare per their natural ordering; mixed-type pairs
   372	    // order by type rank: Number < Text < Boolean. Blank coerces to 0 for
   373	    // Number comparisons and "" for Text. Errors propagate.
   374	    if let Value::Error(e) = lhs {
   375	        return Value::Error(*e);
   376	    }
   377	    if let Value::Error(e) = rhs {
   378	        return Value::Error(*e);
   379	    }
   380	    let cmp = compare_values_excel(lhs, rhs);
   381	    let result_bool = match op {
   382	        Operator::Eq => cmp == std::cmp::Ordering::Equal,
   383	        Operator::Neq => cmp != std::cmp::Ordering::Equal,
   384	        Operator::Lt => cmp == std::cmp::Ordering::Less,
   385	        Operator::Le => matches!(cmp, std::cmp::Ordering::Less | std::cmp::Ordering::Equal),
   386	        Operator::Gt => cmp == std::cmp::Ordering::Greater,
   387	        Operator::Ge => {
   388	            matches!(cmp, std::cmp::Ordering::Greater | std::cmp::Ordering::Equal)
   389	        }
   390	        _ => unreachable!(),
   391	    };
   392	    Value::Boolean(result_bool)
   393	}
   394	
   395	/// Phase 2A.9 audit M2: Excel-canonical comparison ordering. When operands
   396	/// have different types, they're ordered by type rank: Number/Blank < Text <
   397	/// Boolean. Within a type, natural ordering applies. Blank coerces to 0 (for
   398	/// Number comparisons) or "" (for Text); we represent Blank as having the
   399	/// Number rank so `=0=B1` is TRUE for blank B1.
   400	///
   401	/// This replaces `compare_values_phase0`, which returned `None` for mixed
   402	/// types and let downstream code silently degrade to `false` for ordering
   403	/// comparisons — a documented Phase 0 deviation from Excel canon.
   404	fn compare_values_excel(lhs: &Value, rhs: &Value) -> std::cmp::Ordering {
   405	    use std::cmp::Ordering;
   406	    // Same-type comparisons go through the natural ordering.
   407	    match (lhs, rhs) {
   408	        (Value::Number(a), Value::Number(b)) => return a.partial_cmp(b).unwrap_or(Ordering::Equal),
   409	        (Value::Boolean(a), Value::Boolean(b)) => return a.cmp(b),
   410	        (Value::Text(a), Value::Text(b)) => return a.as_ref().cmp(b.as_ref()),
   411	        (Value::Blank, Value::Blank) => return Ordering::Equal,
   412	        _ => {}
   413	    }
   414	    // Blank coerces per the other operand: Blank vs Number → compare as 0;
   415	    // Blank vs Text → compare as "". This matches Excel's behavior for the
   416	    // common `=A1=0` and `=A1=""` idioms.
   417	    match (lhs, rhs) {
   418	        (Value::Blank, Value::Number(b)) => {
   419	            return 0.0_f64.partial_cmp(b).unwrap_or(Ordering::Equal)
   420	        }
   421	        (Value::Number(a), Value::Blank) => return a.partial_cmp(&0.0).unwrap_or(Ordering::Equal),
   422	        (Value::Blank, Value::Text(b)) => return "".cmp(b.as_ref()),
   423	        (Value::Text(a), Value::Blank) => return a.as_ref().cmp(""),
   424	        _ => {}
   425	    }
   426	    // True cross-type: rank by type. Excel canon: Number < Text < Boolean.
   427	    // Blank carries the Number rank (it coerces above before reaching here for
   428	    // Number/Text peers, so this only matters for Blank-vs-Bool).
   429	    fn type_rank(v: &Value) -> u8 {
   430	        match v {
   431	            Value::Number(_) | Value::Blank => 0,
   432	            Value::Text(_) => 1,
   433	            Value::Boolean(_) => 2,
   434	            // Error already short-circuited in eval_compare.
   435	            Value::Error(_) => unreachable!("Error short-circuits in eval_compare"),
   436	        }
   437	    }
   438	    type_rank(lhs).cmp(&type_rank(rhs))
   439	}
   440	
   441	fn eval_unary(op: Operator, operand: Value) -> Value {
   442	    if let Value::Error(e) = operand {
   443	        return Value::Error(e);
   444	    }
   445	    let n = match coercion::to_number_strict(&operand) {
   446	        Ok(n) => n,
   447	        Err(e) => return Value::Error(e),
   448	    };
   449	    let result = match op {
   450	        Operator::Minus => -n,
   451	        Operator::Plus => n,
   452	        Operator::Percent => n / 100.0,
   453	        _ => return Value::Error(ErrorValue::Value),
   454	    };
   455	    match coercion::sanitize_f64(result) {
   456	        Ok(n) => Value::Number(n),
   457	        Err(e) => Value::Error(e),
   458	    }
   459	}

   469	    fn cell_ref(col: u32, row: u32) -> Expr {
   470	        Expr::CellRef(CellAddr {
   471	            sheet: SheetRef::Current,
   472	            col,
   473	            row,
   474	            abs_col: false,
   475	            abs_row: false,
   476	        })
   477	    }
   478	
   479	    fn n(v: f64) -> Expr {
   480	        Expr::Number(v)
   481	    }
   482	
   483	    fn bin(op: Operator, lhs: Expr, rhs: Expr) -> Expr {
   484	        Expr::Binary {
   485	            op,
   486	            lhs: Box::new(lhs),
   487	            rhs: Box::new(rhs),
   488	        }
   489	    }
   490	
   491	    fn eval(expr: &Expr, env: &MapEnv) -> Value {
   492	        let plan = bind(expr, 0).expect("bind");
   493	        eval_scalar(&plan, env)
   494	    }

   207	pub(crate) fn walk_plan_for_deps(plan: &ExprPlan, deps: &mut FormulaDeps) {
   208	    match plan {
   209	        ExprPlan::Number(_) | ExprPlan::Bool(_) | ExprPlan::String(_) => {
   210	            // Leaf literals carry no dependencies.
   211	        }
   212	        ExprPlan::CellRef {
   213	            sheet, row, col, ..
   214	        } => {
   215	            deps.cells.push((*sheet, *row, *col));
   216	        }
   217	        ExprPlan::Binary { lhs, rhs, .. } => {
   218	            walk_plan_for_deps(lhs, deps);
   219	            walk_plan_for_deps(rhs, deps);
   220	        }
   221	        ExprPlan::Unary { operand, .. } => {
   222	            walk_plan_for_deps(operand, deps);
   223	        }
   224	        ExprPlan::Function { name, args } => {
   225	            if is_volatile_function(name) {
   226	                deps.is_volatile = true;
   227	            }
   228	            for arg in args {
   229	                walk_plan_for_deps(arg, deps);
   230	            }
   231	        }
   232	        ExprPlan::AggregateNameRef { name, range } => {
   233	            deps.named_ranges.push((Arc::clone(name), *range));
   234	            deps.names.push(Arc::clone(name));
   235	        }
   236	        // **W5-99 (Phase 4.7.F):** array-literal and error-literal plans
   237	        // have NO dependencies — they're pure constants. Array cells
   238	        // are also restricted to literals (Number/Bool/String/Error),
   239	        // so even walking the inner cells would yield zero deps.
   240	        ExprPlan::Array(_) | ExprPlan::Error(_) => {
   241	            // No deps; leaf literals.
   242	        }
   243	    }
   244	}

   592	/// **W5-99 (Phase 4.7.F):** human-readable variant tag for the
   593	/// `ArrayCellNotLiteral` error message. Used only on the error
   594	/// path so the cost is irrelevant.
   595	fn variant_kind(e: &Expr) -> &'static str {
   596	    match e {
   597	        Expr::Number(_) => "Number",
   598	        Expr::Bool(_) => "Bool",
   599	        Expr::String(_) => "String",
   600	        Expr::Error(_) => "Error",
   601	        Expr::CellRef(_) => "CellRef",
   602	        Expr::RangeRef(_) => "RangeRef",
   603	        Expr::Binary { .. } => "Binary",
   604	        Expr::Unary { .. } => "Unary",
   605	        Expr::Function { .. } => "Function",
   606	        Expr::Array(_) => "Array",
   607	        Expr::Spill(_) => "Spill",
   608	        Expr::NameRef(_) => "NameRef",
   609	    }

/bin/zsh -lc "nl -ba crates/ql-formula-syntax/src/parser.rs | sed -n '455,566p'" in /Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine
 succeeded in 0ms:
   455	    /// array_row    := array_cell (',' array_cell)*
   456	    /// array_cell   := number | string | bool | error_lit | unary_signed_number
   457	    /// ```
   458	    ///
   459	    /// Errors:
   460	    /// - `EmptyArrayLiteral` — `{}` (zero rows).
   461	    /// - `ArrayRowArityMismatch` — row N has different cell count than row 0.
   462	    /// - `InvalidArrayCellToken` — token not in the v1 restricted cell grammar.
   463	    /// - `UnclosedDelimiter { what: "{" }` — end-of-input before `}`.
   464	    fn parse_array_literal(&mut self) -> Result<Expr, ParseError> {
   465	        // Immediate close — empty literal. Reject.
   466	        if matches!(self.peek(), Some(Token::RBrace)) {
   467	            self.advance();
   468	            return Err(ParseError::EmptyArrayLiteral);
   469	        }
   470	        let mut rows: Vec<Vec<Expr>> = Vec::new();
   471	        let mut expected_arity: Option<u32> = None;
   472	        loop {
   473	            // Parse one row.
   474	            let mut row: Vec<Expr> = Vec::new();
   475	            // Each row has at least one cell (we just checked we're not
   476	            // at RBrace; subsequent rows are entered after a Semicolon).
   477	            row.push(self.parse_array_cell()?);
   478	            while let Some(Token::Comma) = self.peek() {
   479	                self.advance();
   480	                row.push(self.parse_array_cell()?);
   481	            }
   482	            // Arity check.
   483	            let row_arity = row.len() as u32;
   484	            match expected_arity {
   485	                None => expected_arity = Some(row_arity),
   486	                Some(expected) if expected != row_arity => {
   487	                    return Err(ParseError::ArrayRowArityMismatch {
   488	                        expected,
   489	                        found: row_arity,
   490	                        row: rows.len() as u32,
   491	                    });
   492	                }
   493	                _ => {}
   494	            }
   495	            rows.push(row);
   496	            // Next: either `;` (more rows), `}` (end), or error.
   497	            match self.advance() {
   498	                Some(Token::Semicolon) => continue,
   499	                Some(Token::RBrace) => return Ok(Expr::Array(rows)),
   500	                Some(other) => {
   501	                    return Err(ParseError::Unexpected {
   502	                        context: "array literal",
   503	                        got: format!("{other:?}"),
   504	                    });
   505	                }
   506	                None => return Err(ParseError::UnclosedDelimiter { what: "{" }),
   507	            }
   508	        }
   509	    }
   510	
   511	    /// **W5-98 (Phase 4.7.D):** parse a single array cell per the
   512	    /// restricted v1 grammar (design § 3.3). Allowed tokens:
   513	    /// - `Token::Number(n)` → `Expr::Number(n)`
   514	    /// - `Token::String(s)` → `Expr::String(s)`
   515	    /// - `Token::Op(Plus|Minus)` + `Token::Number(n)` → folded literal
   516	    /// - `Token::Ident("TRUE"|"FALSE")` → `Expr::Bool`
   517	    /// - `Token::Error(ev)` → `Expr::Error(ev)`
   518	    ///
   519	    /// Anything else (CellRef, BareColumn, BareRow, LParen, LBrace, etc.)
   520	    /// surfaces as `InvalidArrayCellToken`. The binder is the second
   521	    /// line of defense — see `BindError::ArrayRowArityMismatch` for the
   522	    /// matching binder rule in W5-100 (Phase 4.7.F).
   523	    fn parse_array_cell(&mut self) -> Result<Expr, ParseError> {
   524	        let token = self.advance().ok_or(ParseError::UnexpectedEnd {
   525	            context: "array cell",
   526	        })?;
   527	        match token {
   528	            Token::Number(n) => Ok(Expr::Number(n)),
   529	            Token::String(s) => Ok(Expr::String(s)),
   530	            Token::Error(ev) => Ok(Expr::Error(ev)),
   531	            // Unary-signed number: peek for Number; only allow at the
   532	            // immediate next position (no `--5` chains).
   533	            Token::Op(Operator::Minus) => match self.advance() {
   534	                Some(Token::Number(n)) => Ok(Expr::Number(-n)),
   535	                Some(other) => Err(ParseError::InvalidArrayCellToken {
   536	                    got: format!("Op(Minus) followed by {other:?}"),
   537	                }),
   538	                None => Err(ParseError::UnexpectedEnd {
   539	                    context: "array cell after '-'",
   540	                }),
   541	            },
   542	            Token::Op(Operator::Plus) => match self.advance() {
   543	                Some(Token::Number(n)) => Ok(Expr::Number(n)),
   544	                Some(other) => Err(ParseError::InvalidArrayCellToken {
   545	                    got: format!("Op(Plus) followed by {other:?}"),
   546	                }),
   547	                None => Err(ParseError::UnexpectedEnd {
   548	                    context: "array cell after '+'",
   549	                }),
   550	            },
   551	            // TRUE / FALSE come through as `Token::Ident(_)` (4+ letters).
   552	            Token::Ident(name) => {
   553	                let upper = name.to_ascii_uppercase();
   554	                if upper == "TRUE" {
   555	                    Ok(Expr::Bool(true))
   556	                } else if upper == "FALSE" {
   557	                    Ok(Expr::Bool(false))
   558	                } else {
   559	                    Err(ParseError::InvalidArrayCellToken {
   560	                        got: format!("Ident({name:?})"),
   561	                    })
   562	                }
   563	            }
   564	            other => Err(ParseError::InvalidArrayCellToken {
   565	                got: format!("{other:?}"),
   566	            }),

    75	/// (per `Expr: PartialEq`) — modulo Arc<str> identity-vs-content differences and f64
    76	/// NaN-bit-pattern ambiguity — produce identical fingerprints.
    77	///
    78	/// Used by `FormulaRegionNode` to detect "these N cells all have the same formula" at
    79	/// region construction time. Phase 0 doesn't memoize aggregate values (CORR-22 deferred
    80	/// HF-style RangeVertex caching to Phase 4+); the fingerprint is purely for cell-grouping.
    81	pub fn fingerprint(expr: &Expr) -> u64 {
    82	    let mut h = SipHasher24::new_with_keys(FINGERPRINT_SEED_K0, FINGERPRINT_SEED_K1);
    83	    hash_expr(expr, &mut h);
    84	    h.finish()
    85	}
    86	
    87	fn hash_expr(expr: &Expr, h: &mut impl Hasher) {
    88	    // Variant tag bytes are explicit: the discriminant of an enum isn't stable across
    89	    // recompiles, and we want fingerprints to be stable for a given source-formula across
    90	    // engine versions. The byte values are arbitrary but locked.
    91	    match expr {
    92	        Expr::Number(n) => {
    93	            0u8.hash(h);
    94	            n.to_bits().hash(h);
    95	        }
    96	        Expr::String(s) => {
    97	            1u8.hash(h);
    98	            // `Arc<str>` derefs to `&str`; hash by content (the stdlib `str` impl hashes
    99	            // bytes + a length prefix). Arc identity does NOT affect the fingerprint.
   100	            s.as_ref().hash(h);
   101	        }
   102	        Expr::Bool(b) => {
   103	            2u8.hash(h);
   104	            b.hash(h);
   105	        }
   106	        Expr::CellRef(addr) => {
   107	            3u8.hash(h);
   108	            // Phase 2A.10 audit M14: hash CellAddr field-by-field with
   109	            // explicit per-field bytes so the fingerprint doesn't depend on
   110	            // the derived Hash for Option<SheetId> (which writes a
   111	            // discriminant byte for None vs Some).
   112	            hash_cell_addr(addr, h);
   113	        }
   114	        Expr::RangeRef(r) => {
   115	            4u8.hash(h);
   116	            // Phase 2A.10 audit M14: explicit per-variant tag bytes for
   117	            // RangeRef. Derived Hash would auto-assign discriminants
   118	            // (Cells=0, WholeColumn=1, WholeRow=2) based on source order;
   119	            // explicit tags make the fingerprint immune to source reorders.
   120	            hash_range_ref(r, h);
   121	        }
   122	        Expr::Binary { op, lhs, rhs } => {
   123	            5u8.hash(h);
   124	            hash_operator(op, h);
   125	            hash_expr(lhs, h);
   126	            hash_expr(rhs, h);
   127	        }
   128	        Expr::Unary { op, operand } => {
   129	            6u8.hash(h);
   130	            hash_operator(op, h);
   131	            hash_expr(operand, h);
   132	        }
   133	        Expr::Function { name, args } => {
   134	            7u8.hash(h);
   135	            // Function name normalization is the parser's job (uppercase per Excel
   136	            // canon); the fingerprint hashes whatever was actually stored. Two
   137	            // distinct-cased `Arc<str>::from("SUM")` vs `Arc<str>::from("sum")` will
   138	            // fingerprint differently — that's a parser-level invariant problem if it
   139	            // ever happens.
   140	            name.as_ref().hash(h);
   141	            for arg in args {
   142	                hash_expr(arg, h);
   143	            }
   144	            // Argument count: `f(a, b)` and `f(a, b, c)` must fingerprint differently
   145	            // even when the first two args match. Without the explicit length, a
   146	            // `Function { name, args: [a, b] }` could collide with `Function { name,
   147	            // args: [a, b, junk] }` if `junk` hashed to zero bytes (unlikely but the
   148	            // length-suffix is cheap insurance).
   149	            args.len().hash(h);
   150	        }
   151	        Expr::Array(rows) => {
   152	            8u8.hash(h);
   153	            for row in rows {
   154	                for cell in row {
   155	                    hash_expr(cell, h);
   156	                }
   157	                row.len().hash(h);
   158	            }
   159	            rows.len().hash(h);
   160	        }
   161	        Expr::Spill(inner) => {
   162	            9u8.hash(h);
   163	            hash_expr(inner, h);
   164	        }
   165	        Expr::NameRef(name) => {
   166	            // Phase 2A.1: defined-name reference. Hash by content (Arc-identity
   167	            // independence) so two cells with the same name fingerprint identically.
   168	            10u8.hash(h);
   169	            name.as_ref().hash(h);
   170	        }
   171	        Expr::Error(ev) => {
   172	            // **W5-98 (Phase 4.7.D):** error literal. Hash by the
   173	            // sigil string so two cells with the same error fingerprint
   174	            // identically and so the fingerprint is stable across
   175	            // potential future ErrorValue discriminant reorders.
   176	            11u8.hash(h);
   177	            ev.sigil().hash(h);
   178	        }
   179	    }
   180	}

 succeeded in 0ms:
   312	    /// SipHash-2-4 algorithm with seed `(0, 0)` + the explicit-byte tagging
   313	    /// established in this module. Any algorithm change OR variant
   314	    /// discriminant remap trips this test.
   315	    ///
   316	    /// # Regenerating
   317	    ///
   318	    /// If a seed rotation or algorithm change is intentional:
   319	    /// 1. Set every value below to `0`.
   320	    /// 2. Run `cargo test golden_fingerprints_locked -- --nocapture` —
   321	    ///    the test will fail and print the actual fingerprints via the
   322	    ///    helper `print_golden_for_regeneration`.
   323	    /// 3. Paste the printed values back into the array.
   324	    /// 4. Bump any persisted-fingerprint schema version (Engine Phase 3+
   325	    ///    calcgraph cache layer; see `docs/MASTER-PLAN.md` Phase 3.6).

 succeeded in 0ms:
   470	    ///
   471	    /// Returns the evaluated value, or a `RuntimeError` if any pipeline stage fails.
   472	    /// On error, the workbook is unchanged (no partial writes).
   473	    ///
   474	    /// `formula_text` is the formula body without the leading `=`. The Sheet must
   475	    /// already exist.
   476	    pub fn set_formula(
   477	        &mut self,
   478	        sheet: SheetId,
   479	        row: RowId,
   480	        col: ColId,
   481	        formula_text: impl Into<Arc<str>>,
   482	    ) -> Result<Value, RuntimeError> {
   483	        // Phase 2A.7 audit H1 (was 2A.6 L4 sheet-only): validate sheet AND
   484	        // row/col bounds up front. Without these checks, out-of-grid cells
   485	        // sneak past lex/parse/bind and panic inside `Sheet::put` (which
   486	        // asserts `row <= MAX_ROW && col <= MAX_COLUMN`).
   487	        validate_cell(self.workbook, sheet, row, col)?;
   488	
   489	        let formula_text = formula_text.into();
   490	        // Phase 2B.3: consult the bind-plan cache first. The key includes
   491	        // the current NameTable generation so a name registration since
   492	        // the last bind produces a miss → re-bind against the new table.
   493	        // Cached `Arc<ExprPlan>` is shared with `recompute_all`, so a
   494	        // subsequent recompute of this cell skips lex/parse/bind entirely.
   495	        let name_gen = self.workbook.names().generation();
   496	        let cache_key = PlanCacheKey {
   497	            text: Arc::clone(&formula_text),
   498	            sheet,
   499	            name_gen,
   500	        };
   501	        let plan: Arc<crate::plan::ExprPlan> =
   502	            self.plan_cache
   503	                .get_or_insert::<_, RuntimeError>(cache_key, || {
   504	                    let tokens = lex(formula_text.as_ref())?;
   505	                    let expr = parse(tokens)?;
   506	                    // Phase 2A.1 (2026-05-12): bind against the workbook's
   507	                    // NameTable so `Expr::NameRef` resolves against defined
   508	                    // names. W5-92 (Phase 4.6.D): pass `&Workbook` for names
   509	                    // so the two-tier sheet-then-workbook chain fires.
   510	                    Ok(bind_with_names_and_sheets(
   511	                        &expr,
   512	                        sheet,
   513	                        self.workbook,
   514	                        self.workbook,
   515	                    )?)
   516	                })?;
   517	
   518	        // Evaluate against the current workbook state. The env borrows immutably; we
   519	        // drop it before taking the mutable borrow for the write.
   520	        // Phase 3.6 (W5-39): if a calcgraph session is attached, route
   521	        // the eval through `eval_scalar_with_cache` so aggregate-range
   522	        // results land in the session's cache (next recompute on an
   523	        // unchanged range hits without re-scanning).
   524	        let value = {
   525	            let env = WorkbookEnv::new(self.workbook);
   526	            match self.graph.as_deref() {
   527	                Some(session) => crate::scalar::eval_scalar_with_cache(
   528	                    plan.as_ref(),
   529	                    &env,
   530	                    self.registry,
   531	                    session.aggregate_cache(),
   532	                ),
   533	                None => eval_scalar_with_registry(plan.as_ref(), &env, self.registry),
   534	            }
   535	        };
   536	
   537	        // Phase 2A.3.b op-log emission, BEFORE mutation. If append fails, no
   538	        // mutation; workbook stays consistent. We emit `PutFormula` only — the
   539	        // evaluated value isn't recorded because replay re-derives it via
   540	        // `WorkbookRuntime::recompute_all` (see replay.rs module docs).
   541	        if let Some(oplog) = self.oplog.as_deref_mut() {
   542	            oplog.append(Op::PutFormula {
   543	                sheet,
   544	                row,
   545	                col,
   546	                text: formula_text.as_ref().to_owned(),
   547	            })?;
   548	        }
   549	
   550	        // Persist formula text + evaluated value. Phase 3.5 (CORR-25):
   551	        // a formula's output goes to the COMPUTED overlay, not the user
   552	        // overlay; if the cell previously held a user-typed value, we
   553	        // clear that lane first so the user-first read cascade doesn't
   554	        // mask the new formula output. `put_at` can't fail because
   555	        // `validate_cell` at the top of this method already rejected
   556	        // out-of-range (sheet, row, col).
   557	        self.workbook.clear_user_at(sheet, row, col);
   558	        self.workbook
   559	            .put_computed_at(sheet, row, col, value.clone());
   560	        self.workbook.put_formula(sheet, row, col, formula_text);
   561	
   562	        // Phase 3.1: notify calcgraph after the workbook mutation
   563	        // succeeds. Phase 3.2 (2026-05-12): pass the already-bound
   564	        // `ExprPlan` straight from the PlanCache so the hook walks it
   565	        // for cell/range/name + volatile deps without re-binding. The
   566	        // plan is shared by Arc; the hook only needs `&ExprPlan`.
   567	        if let Some(g) = self.graph.as_deref_mut() {
   568	            g.on_set_formula(sheet, row, col, plan.as_ref());
   569	        }
   570	
   571	        Ok(value)
   572	    }
   573

   575	    /// association at the cell — typing a value over a formula cell deletes the

codex

commit 5fc6431b0d7f859546c5f453bdd5ff1f31b1ea02
Author: steppen0mad <nurtaymergaliyev@gmail.com>
Date:   Thu May 14 04:59:35 2026 +0100

    W5-99 / Engine Phase 4.7.F — Binder Expr::Array + Expr::Error lowering
    
    Sixth implementation sub-phase of Phase 4.7. Lowers the parser's
    `Expr::Array(Vec<Vec<Expr>>)` + `Expr::Error(ErrorValue)` into new
    `ExprPlan` variants that the runtime + calcgraph already handle.
    
    New `ExprPlan` variants in `crates/ql-exec/src/plan.rs`:
    - `ExprPlan::Array(Vec<Vec<ExprPlan>>)` — row-major, uniform arity.
      Inner cells restricted to literal-kind plans (Number / Bool /
      String / Error) per design § 3.3.
    - `ExprPlan::Error(ErrorValue)` — error-sigil literal. Mechanical
      lowering from `Expr::Error(_)`.
    
    New `BindError` variants (defense in depth on the public AST):
    - `ArrayRowArityMismatch { expected, found, row }` — ragged Array.
      Parser already rejects via `ParseError::ArrayRowArityMismatch`,
      but `Expr::Array` is a public variant — tests/tools can construct
      ragged shapes directly. Codex MEDIUM-5 in the W5-94 design review.
    - `ArrayCellNotLiteral { row, col, got }` — non-literal cell. Parser
      rejects at parse time via `InvalidArrayCellToken`; binder mirror
      for direct AST construction. Includes a `variant_kind` helper for
      human-readable variant tags in the error.
    
    Binder lowering in `bind_with_context`:
    - `Expr::Error(ev)` → `ExprPlan::Error(ev)`.
    - `Expr::Array(rows)` → `ExprPlan::Array(bound_rows)` element-wise.
      Validates uniform row arity + restricted cell-kind set. Zero-row
      AST surfaces as `BindError::UnsupportedVariant`.
    
    Downstream `ExprPlan` callers updated:
    - `calcgraph_session::walk_plan_for_deps` — `Array` and `Error`
      have no dependencies (pure leaf literals).
    - `scalar::eval_scalar` AND `scalar::eval_scalar_with_cache` —
      `Error(ev)` returns `Value::Error(ev)`; `Array(_)` in scalar
      context returns `Value::Error(ErrorValue::Calc)` per design § 6.3
      (Codex HIGH-2). Cell-boundary spill path is W5-102 / Phase 4.7.J;
      until then, every array-in-scalar evaluation produces #CALC!.
    
    Tests (+13 from 2065 → 2078):
    - 8 binder tests (plan.rs):
      - Error literal lowering.
      - Array literal element-wise lowering (4 cell kinds).
      - Ragged-array → ArrayRowArityMismatch.
      - Non-literal cell → ArrayCellNotLiteral (CellRef + nested Array).
      - Zero-row → UnsupportedVariant.
      - 1x1 + 3x2 array lowering.
    - 5 eval-site tests (scalar.rs):
      - ExprPlan::Error → Value::Error via both eval entry points.
      - ExprPlan::Array → Value::Error(Calc) via both eval entry points.
      - 1 + #N/A end-to-end binary propagation.
    
    Gates (all 7 green):
    - fmt clean, clippy -D warnings clean
    - cargo test --workspace: 2078 passed / 0 failed
    - check-build-flags / check-cargo-lock-pins / check-multiversion-
      clones: OK
    - cargo audit: 1 allowed warning (atomic-polyfill, pre-existing)
    
    Next: Sonnet pull-up per design plan. Then W5-100+ for the
    evaluator-side EvalResult::Array materialization (Phase 4.7.G).
    
    Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>

+    /// **W5-99 (Phase 4.7.F):** array literal lowered from `Expr::Array`.
+    /// Row-major; outer `Vec` is rows, inner `Vec` is cells. Element-arity
+    /// is invariant — every row has the same length. The binder enforces
+    /// this (`BindError::ArrayRowArityMismatch`) on every public input AND
+    /// the parser already enforces it (`ParseError::ArrayRowArityMismatch`);
+    /// defense in depth — the binder can't trust the AST because
+    /// `Expr::Array` is a public variant that tests / tools can construct
+    /// directly with ragged shapes.
+    ///
+    /// Inner `ExprPlan`s are restricted to the array-cell subset per
+    /// design § 3.3: `Number` / `Bool` / `String` / `Error`. Cell-refs,
+    /// function calls, and nested arrays are NOT permitted in v1 (already
+    /// rejected at parse time via `ParseError::InvalidArrayCellToken`).
+    /// The binder validates the same subset as defense in depth.
+    ///
+    /// Eval-site dispatch: Phase 4.7.G (W5-101). The runtime materializes
+    /// an `ArrayValue` from this plan and routes to either the cell-
+    /// boundary spill path (cell context) or `#CALC!` (scalar context),
+    /// per design § 6.3.
+    Array(Vec<Vec<ExprPlan>>),
+
+    /// **W5-99 (Phase 4.7.F):** error-sigil literal lowered from
+    /// `Expr::Error(ev)`. The evaluator returns `Value::Error(ev)` directly;
+    /// the lowering is mechanical (no resolution, no context). Used by
+    /// `=#REF!` formulas, `IFERROR(..., #N/A)` fallback args, and error
+    /// literals inside `Expr::Array` cells.
+    Error(ErrorValue),
 }

+    /// (`ParseError::ArrayRowArityMismatch`), but `Expr::Array` is a

+    #[error("array literal row {row} has {found} cell(s), expected {expected} (matching row 0)")]
+    ArrayRowArityMismatch { expected: u32, found: u32, row: u32 },
+
+    /// **W5-99 (Phase 4.7.F):** `Expr::Array` contains a cell that
+    /// isn't in the bind-allowed subset (Number / Bool / String /
+    /// Error). The parser already restricts cell tokens at parse
+    /// time, but defense-in-depth applies to tests / tools that

+    ArrayCellNotLiteral {

+    },

+        // **W5-99 (Phase 4.7.F):** lower `Expr::Array` to
+        // `ExprPlan::Array`. Per design § 5, this is element-wise +
+        // enforces uniform row arity AND the restricted cell-kind subset
+        // (Number / Bool / String / Error). The parser already validates
+        // both; this is defense in depth against direct AST construction.
+        Expr::Array(rows) => {
+            if rows.is_empty() {
+                // The parser rejects `{}` with EmptyArrayLiteral, but a
+                // direct AST construction with `vec![]` could land here.
+                // No dedicated bind-error variant — surface as a clean
+                // UnsupportedVariant for the degenerate construction.
+                return Err(BindError::UnsupportedVariant(
+                    "Expr::Array with zero rows is not constructible from source; \
+                     direct AST construction with an empty Vec is rejected",
+                ));
+            }
+            let expected_arity = rows[0].len() as u32;
+            let mut bound_rows: Vec<Vec<ExprPlan>> = Vec::with_capacity(rows.len());
+            for (row_idx, row) in rows.iter().enumerate() {
+                let found_arity = row.len() as u32;
+                if found_arity != expected_arity {
+                    return Err(BindError::ArrayRowArityMismatch {
+                        expected: expected_arity,
+                        found: found_arity,
+                        row: row_idx as u32,
+                    });
+                }
+                let mut bound_row: Vec<ExprPlan> = Vec::with_capacity(row.len());
+                for (col_idx, cell) in row.iter().enumerate() {
+                    let bound_cell = match cell {
+                        Expr::Number(n) => ExprPlan::Number(*n),
+                        Expr::Bool(b) => ExprPlan::Bool(*b),
+                        Expr::String(s) => ExprPlan::String(s.clone()),
+                        Expr::Error(ev) => ExprPlan::Error(*ev),
+                        // Per design § 3.3 / parser restriction: only the
+                        // four literal kinds are allowed inside an array
+                        // cell. Reject everything else loudly so a future
+                        // test that constructs `Expr::Array(vec![vec![
+                        // Expr::CellRef(_)]])` directly surfaces a clean
+                        // error rather than silently producing a corrupt
+                        // ExprPlan.
+                        other => {
+                            return Err(BindError::ArrayCellNotLiteral {
+                                row: row_idx as u32,
+                                col: col_idx as u32,
+                                got: variant_kind(other),
+                            });
+                        }
+                    };
+                    bound_row.push(bound_cell);
+                }
+                bound_rows.push(bound_row);
+            }
+            Ok(ExprPlan::Array(bound_rows))
+        }
+
         Expr::Spill(_) => Err(BindError::UnsupportedVariant(
             "Spill-range-ref `A1#` syntax is Phase 4.9",
         )),
-        Expr::Error(_) => Err(BindError::UnsupportedVariant(
-            "Error literals — binder support lands in Phase 4.7.F (W5-100)",
-        )),

+        // No resolution, no context. The evaluator returns
+        // `Value::Error(ev)` directly.

+/// **W5-99 (Phase 4.7.F):** human-readable variant tag for the
+/// `ArrayCellNotLiteral` error message. Used only on the error
+/// path so the cost is irrelevant.
+fn variant_kind(e: &Expr) -> &'static str {
+    match e {
+        Expr::Number(_) => "Number",
+        Expr::Bool(_) => "Bool",
+        Expr::String(_) => "String",
+        Expr::Error(_) => "Error",
+        Expr::CellRef(_) => "CellRef",
+        Expr::RangeRef(_) => "RangeRef",
+        Expr::Binary { .. } => "Binary",
+        Expr::Unary { .. } => "Unary",
+        Expr::Function { .. } => "Function",
+        Expr::Array(_) => "Array",
+        Expr::Spill(_) => "Spill",
+        Expr::NameRef(_) => "NameRef",
+    }
+}

**Independent Review: Phase 4.7.F / W5-99**

Scope checked: design §5 and §6.3, prior Codex 4.7.F-relevant findings, `git show 5fc6431b0d7 --stat`, and the W5-99 changes in `plan.rs`, `scalar.rs`, and `calcgraph_session.rs`.

I could not independently run tests: `cargo` is not available in this environment (`zsh: command not found: cargo`). Source-level verification only.

**Claim Verdicts**

1. PASS: `ExprPlan` now has `Array(Vec<Vec<ExprPlan>>)` and `Error(ErrorValue)` variants in `crates/ql-exec/src/plan.rs:82` and `crates/ql-exec/src/plan.rs:103`.

2. PASS: Binder lowers `Expr::Error(ev)` mechanically to `ExprPlan::Error(ev)` at `crates/ql-exec/src/plan.rs:465`.

3. PASS: Binder lowers `Expr::Array(rows)` element-wise and preserves row-major `Vec<Vec<_>>` shape at `crates/ql-exec/src/plan.rs:410`; no flattening, scalarizing, or row collapsing is present.

4. PASS: Uniform row arity is validated with `BindError::ArrayRowArityMismatch` at `crates/ql-exec/src/plan.rs:421`.

5. PASS: Literal-kind validation rejects every non-`Number` / `Bool` / `String` / `Error` AST cell through `ArrayCellNotLiteral` at `crates/ql-exec/src/plan.rs:433`.

6. PASS: Binder does not accept `Expr::NameRef` inside array cells; it falls into the `other` rejection arm and reports `got: "NameRef"` via `variant_kind` at `crates/ql-exec/src/plan.rs:446` and `crates/ql-exec/src/plan.rs:608`. This matches the parser, which rejects non-TRUE/FALSE identifiers in array cells at `crates/ql-formula-syntax/src/parser.rs:551`.

7. FLAG - MEDIUM: Empty/degenerate direct-AST array validation is incomplete and uses the wrong error class. `rows.is_empty()` returns generic `BindError::UnsupportedVariant` at `crates/ql-exec/src/plan.rs:410`, while the parser has a dedicated `ParseError::EmptyArrayLiteral` path at `crates/ql-formula-syntax/src/parser.rs:464`; additionally, `Expr::Array(vec![vec![]])` is accepted because `expected_arity` can be `0` at `crates/ql-exec/src/plan.rs:421`, even though the parser grammar requires at least one cell per row. Suggested fix: add a dedicated `BindError::EmptyArrayLiteral` or `BindError::DegenerateArrayLiteral` and reject `rows.is_empty() || rows[0].is_empty()` before binding.

8. PASS: Scalar eval returns `Value::Error(ErrorValue::Calc)` for `ExprPlan::Array(_)` in both evaluator entry points at `crates/ql-exec/src/scalar.rs:94` and `crates/ql-exec/src/scalar.rs:273`, matching design §6.3 for scalar contexts.

9. PASS with future-test requirement: The current cell-boundary interim state is sound because the spill path is explicitly deferred and `WorkbookRuntime::set_formula` still routes root formulas through scalar eval at `crates/ql-exec/src/workbook_runtime.rs:524`. In 4.7.J, root array/spill detection must intercept before those eval calls; otherwise the failure mode is visible `#CALC!`, not silent scalar truncation. Add runtime tests for `={1,2}` at a cell root spilling rather than returning `#CALC!`.

10. PASS: `ExprPlan::Error(ev)` evaluates to `Value::Error(ev)` in both scalar eval paths at `crates/ql-exec/src/scalar.rs:85` and `crates/ql-exec/src/scalar.rs:268`.

11. PASS: `walk_plan_for_deps` treats `ExprPlan::Array(_)` and `ExprPlan::Error(_)` as no-dep leaf literals at `crates/ql-exec/src/calcgraph_session.rs:236`.

12. PASS today / known Phase 4.10 gap: Treating arrays as no-deps is correct while array cells are literal-only, but once Phase 4.10 allows refs inside array literals, `walk_plan_for_deps` must descend into `ExprPlan::Array` rows instead of treating the array as a leaf. Suggested future fix: change the arm at `crates/ql-exec/src/calcgraph_session.rs:240` to walk each inner cell when binder restrictions are lifted.

13. PASS: `variant_kind` is exhaustive today. It matches all 12 current `Expr` variants without a wildcard at `crates/ql-exec/src/plan.rs:595`, so a future `Expr` variant will produce a compile error rather than silently falling through.

14. PASS: `ExprPlan` changes do not affect calcgraph fingerprinting. The fingerprint module hashes `ql_formula_syntax::Expr`, not `ExprPlan`, at `crates/ql-calcgraph/src/fingerprint.rs:81`; `Expr::Array` and `Expr::Error` already have explicit tags at `crates/ql-calcgraph/src/fingerprint.rs:151` and `crates/ql-calcgraph/src/fingerprint.rs:171`, with golden coverage at `crates/ql-calcgraph/src/fingerprint.rs:543` and `crates/ql-calcgraph/src/fingerprint.rs:563`.

15. PASS source-only: The claimed 13 new tests are present in source: 8 binder tests in `crates/ql-exec/src/plan.rs:942` and 5 eval-site tests in `crates/ql-exec/src/scalar.rs:1200`. I could not verify the “2078 workspace tests total” run because `cargo` is unavailable here.

**Bottom Line**

W5-99 is mostly correct against the Phase 4.7.F contract. The one substantive issue is direct-AST degenerate array handling: zero rows use a generic `UnsupportedVariant`, and zero-column rows are not rejected at all. That should be tightened before later spill materialization code has to deal with impossible literal shapes.
